If so, what control?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-14: keep it, Admin-authored, single Approver, behind a workspace feature flag defaulting to off. Revisit after Phase 1.** | Keep + dual approval: maximum expressiveness, real sandboxing burden (NFR-473). Keep + admin-only: less flexible but far fewer reviewers to train. Drop: the declarative checks cover ~95 % of real rules; the last 5 % becomes a feature request. |  |  |  |
| OQ-559 | open question |  | ~~Does the `sql` custom check earn its keep, and if kept, is dual approval the right control or should it be admin-only?~~ **Decided 2026-08-14: kept, Admin-authored, single Approver, behind a workspace flag defaulting to off (§4.5). Revisit after Phase 1.** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-560 | open question |  | ~~Where do IBNR / claims-development adjustments live?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-14: out of scope — the platform consumes developed data supplied by the user, and warns about immature periods.** | Preparation step (developed amounts in the dataset), modelling offset, or out of scope (user supplies developed data). Currently only `VR-ACT-14` warns about immature periods. |  |  |  |
| OQ-560 | open question |  | ~~Where do IBNR / claims-development adjustments live — a preparation step producing developed claim amounts, a modelling offset, or out of scope entirely (user supplies developed data)? `VR-ACT-14` currently only *warns*. | ~~ **Decided 2026-08-14: out of scope — the user supplies developed data; `VR-ACT-14` warns about immature periods (§1.2).** |  |  |  |
| OQ-561 | open question |  | ~~Ship ONS/ABI reference data, or only loaders?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-14: loaders for every source; ship data only where the licence is unambiguously permissive. Never ship ABI group tables.** | Shipping is far better UX but the licences (ONS OGL is permissive; ABI vehicle groups are **not** freely redistributable) differ per source and get us wrong quickly. |  |  |  |
| OQ-561 | open question |  | ~~Should the platform hold the ONS/ABI reference sets as shipped data, given their licensing terms, or only ship loaders and require the user to supply the files? | ~~ **Decided 2026-08-14: loaders for every source; rows shipped only under an unambiguous licence; ABI group tables never (FR-72).** |  |  |  |
| OQ-562 | open question |  | ~~What does a **first** failing validation leave a Dataset Version as?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-15: `failed`.** | Nothing sets `failed` today, so the version rests in `validating` — a transient state that every screen showing a status reads as "still running". Found by driving the exit demo, not by a test: no requirement named the state, so no marker could be missing. **`failed`**: the enum and `FAILED → VALIDATING` exist for exactly this, and the word tells a reader the run finished and the answer was no. **`draft`**: what FR-53 does when a *validated* version is re-validated and fails — consistent, but it loses the distinction between "not yet validated" and "validated and refused". |  |  |  |
| OQ-562 | open question |  | ~~Nothing in the platform ever sets a Dataset Version to `failed`. `DatasetStatus.FAILED` exists in the enum and in `VALID_DATASET_TRANSITIONS`, and no code path transitions to it — so a version whose first validation fails rests in **`validating`**, which every screen reads as "still running". FR-27 uses `failed` for a broken ingestion run and FR-53 sends a *re-validated* version back to `draft`; the first-failure case was specified nowhere. Found by exercising Phase 1a's exit demo, 2026-08-15.~~ **Decided and delivered 2026-08-15: `failed`** (FR-52). |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-563 | open question |  | ~~`sample`, `filter`, `join` and `aggregate` derived versions still inherit their parent's data rather than being produced from it — should they be materialised, and when?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-17: materialise all four, each in the slice that first needs it, and refuse them until then.** | Raised 2026-08-16 (WK-661, diagnostics) by needing an honest holdout. `dataset.derive` recorded the operation and set `child.tables = parent.tables`, conflating FR-74's "inherits schema, Data Dictionary and Rule Set" with inheriting the *rows*. **`split` is materialised now** (FR-77) because a holdout containing every training row produces diagnostics that look excellent and mean nothing. The other four were left alone: each has its own correctness surface — stratified and exposure-weighted sampling, the restricted filter grammar, join keys and grain — and doing them under a modelling slice would be a large DATA change with no caller yet. The cost of leaving them: a 1 % sample presently holds 100 % of the rows, silently. |  |  |  |
| OQ-563 | open question |  | ~~`sample`, `filter`, `join` and `aggregate` derived versions inherit their parent's rows rather than being produced from them — a 1 % sample holds 100 % of them.~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-17: materialise all four, each in the slice that first needs it, and refuse them until then rather than leaving the silent version.** Specified as FR-78 and the refusal delivered the same day; `split` remains the one materialised operation (FR-77). Owner: WK-665 for `sample`; `filter`, `join` and `aggregate` unowned. Raised 2026-08-16 (WK-661). |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-564 | open question |  | ~~Acknowledgement granularity — per rule per report, or a time-boxed standing acknowledgement?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-14: per report, as FR-47 already specifies, plus a carry-forward affordance that still requires an explicit act.** | Per report (FR-47): strongest evidence trail, but a known seasonal warning is re-justified every month, breeding rubber-stamping. Standing: less fatigue, weaker evidence, risk of a stale acknowledgement hiding a real change. |  |  |  |
| OQ-564 | open question |  | ~~Is `warn` acknowledgement per-rule-per-report the right granularity, or should an actuary be able to pre-approve a recurring known warning for a defined period (with expiry) to avoid acknowledgement fatigue? | ~~ **Decided 2026-08-14: per report as FR-47 specifies, plus a pre-fill affordance that still requires an explicit, separately audited act.** |  |  |  |
| OQ-565 | open question |  | ~~`01` §5.3 asks the dataset list to display a status badge, a last-validated date and an owner. `Dataset` carries none of the three: status and `validation_report_id` live on `DatasetVersion`, and ownership is only implied by `workspace_id`. Does `Dataset` gain the three fields, or does §5.3 mean the latest version's status and validated-at, plus a workspace-level owner?~~ | Raised 2026-08-18 (WK-661). Recorded as an unowned divergence in `docs/roadmap.md` since 2026-08-15 and built around in silence, which is the `CLAUDE.md` §0 failure dated amendment notes exist to prevent. `Dataset` is a *container*: `01` §4.1's contract shows no status either, so the divergence is between §5.3 and §4.1 as much as between spec and code. |  |  |  |
| OQ-565 | open question |  | ~~§5.3 asks the dataset list to display a status badge, a last-validated date and an owner. `Dataset` carries none of the three: status and `validation_report_id` live on `DatasetVersion`, and ownership is only implied by `workspace_id`. Does `Dataset` gain the three fields, or does §5.3 mean the latest version's status and validated-at, plus a workspace-level owner?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-19: two of the three are projections of the latest versions, one is a new field.** Specified as FR-55 (`latest_version_status` and `last_validated_at`, derived by the list endpoint, never stored) and FR-82 (`Dataset.owner_id`, explicit, not derived from `workspace_id`). Neither is delivered; both are WK-664's, with the trigger named in the requirements. Raised 2026-08-18 (WK-661). |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-566 | open question |  | ~~FR-60 asks for "top-20 levels **by exposure and by count**", and the platform now produces one list — selected by count, each level carrying its `exposure_years` (FR-66). Should there also be a second, exposure-ordered selection; and should `VR-DST-1`'s PSI weight by exposure rather than by count?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-19: defer both, together, until a consumer needs an exposure-ordered view.** FR-60 amended; the deferral, its trigger and its unowned status are FR-67. | Raised 2026-08-19 (WK-661, the `top_levels` slice). **The two halves are one question and must be answered together.** A level with few policies but large exposure still never enters the top 20, because selection remains by count — so an exposure-ordered view and an exposure-weighted PSI would need the same underlying selection to be meaningful. Answering only the PSI half would leave the check weighting a set of levels chosen on the other basis, which is worse than either consistent answer. `psi_from_weights` already takes arbitrary weights and needs no signature change; `VR-DST-8` (mix-shift-exposure) already asks for PSI on exposure for a *declared key factor*, so part of the need is met by a different rule. |  |  |  |
| OQ-566 | open question |  | ~~FR-60 asks for "top-20 levels by exposure and by count", and the platform produces one list selected by count with `exposure_years` carried per level (FR-66). Should there be a second, exposure-ordered selection, and should `VR-DST-1`'s PSI weight by exposure rather than count? The two are one question — an exposure-weighted PSI over a count-selected level set is meaningless.~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-19: defer both, together, until a consumer needs an exposure-ordered view.** FR-60 amended to say the platform produces one count-selected list; the deferral, its trigger and its unowned status are FR-67. Raised 2026-08-19 (WK-661). |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-567 | open question |  | ~~**What is an Offending Sample entry — an opaque string or a keyed object?** FR-49 and the §2 glossary both say "primary keys of rows" and neither chooses an encoding; §4.6's only example prints `"offending_sample": []`, which settles nothing. The two written sides disagree and have since `validation-report` first gained a generated side (2026-08-24, W32-11): the model says an array of `string`, the authored contract an array of `object`.~~ | **(a) String, as the code emits today.** `_sample` in `pricing_core.data.validate` joins the key values with `\|`. Cheap, already shipped, and readable in a log line — but it is an encoding no specification defines: there is no escaping, so a key containing `\|` is ambiguous; `None` is rendered `""` and cannot be told from an empty string; and the column names are dropped, so a composite key cannot be reassembled without knowing the dataset's key order. A sample exists to be traced back to rows, and this one cannot always be. **(b) Keyed object, as the contract declares.** `{"col": "value"}` is self-describing, unambiguous under composite keys, and distinguishes null from empty. But the contract's declaration is today a bare `{"type": "object"}` with no properties, which constrains nothing a validator could check, so (b) is only real once the item shape is written out. Cost: `pricing-core`'s validation engine, roughly twelve assertions across `test_validate.py` and `test_catalogue.py`, the published contract, the generated frontend type, and §4.6's example. **(c) Keep both — string for display, object for tracing.** Rejected on sight: two encodings of one field is the divergence ADR-704 exists to remove. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (b) — the Offending Sample item is a keyed object, the item shape written out** |  |  |  |
| OQ-567 | open question |  | ~~**What is an Offending Sample entry — an opaque string or a keyed object?**~~ Raised 2026-08-24 (W32-11), the day `validation-report` first gained a generated side and the two written sides could be compared at all. FR-49 and the §2 glossary both say "primary keys of rows" without choosing an encoding, and §4.6's example prints an empty array, so neither settles it. The model produces an array of `string` — key values joined with `\|` by `_sample` in `pricing_core.data.validate`, with no escaping, `None` rendered as the empty string, and the column names dropped — while this module's own contract declares an array of `object`. The recommendation is the keyed object with its item shape written out, because a sample exists to be traced back to rows and the string form is lossy in three independent ways at that job; it is recommended rather than done because the change spans the validation engine, its tests, the published contract, the generated frontend type and §4.6's example. Options and the full argument are in [`../open-questions.md`](open-questions.md). Until it is decided, the contract guard's type comparison is pinned at that one path with a companion test that goes red when the pin stops earning its place. Owner: maintainer. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (b) — the Offending Sample item is a keyed object, the item shape written out** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-568 | open question |  | ~~**`DatasetVersion` and its published contract disagree on 22 of their 48 paths, and every comparison passes.** Measured 2026-08-24 (W32-11) on the day the slug first gained a generated side. 26 paths are present on both and **none of them disagrees**; the divergence is entirely one-sided and therefore entirely outside what the guard compares (`OQ-649`). The contract promises 17 paths the model does not carry — `slug`, `description`, `created_by`, `updated_at`, `archived_at`, `currency`, `labels`, `parent_id`, `schema_version`, and the object forms `derived_from.{operation,params,parent_version_id}`, `period_covered.{from,to}` and `source_fingerprint.{kind,value,extracted_at}`. The model carries 5 the contract does not: `derived_from`, `period_from`, `period_to`, `source_fingerprint` and `tables.[].source_names`. Three of those are the same concept in a different shape — the model holds a scalar where the contract declares an object — so the pair is not merely incomplete on one side, it is structurally different on three fields. A Dataset Version is a Phase-1a artifact that `00` §2 makes the immutable thing modelling references, so this is a live disagreement, not a Phase-2 stub.~~ | **(a) The contract is ahead of the model and the model catches up.** Most likely correct for the flat fields — `slug`, `created_by`, `currency` and the rest read as specified-but-unbuilt rather than as contract error, and `01` describes them. Cost is a data-model change plus a migration. **(b) The contract is wrong and is trimmed to what exists.** Fast and wrong in the governed direction: it deletes the specification to make the tooling agree, which `CLAUDE.md` §0 names explicitly as the thing not to do. **(c) Split the three structural fields from the fourteen flat ones and answer them separately** — the flat ones by (a), the structural ones (`derived_from`, `period_*`, `source_fingerprint`) as a genuine shape question, since a scalar and an object are two designs and not two stages of one. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (c) — 14 flat fields adopt the contract's scalars; derived_from, period_* and source_fingerprint adopt the contract's object forms** |  |  |  |
| OQ-568 | open question |  | ~~**`DatasetVersion` and its published contract disagree on 22 of their 48 paths, and every comparison passes.**~~ Measured 2026-08-24 (W32-11), the day the slug first gained a generated side. Of 26 paths present on both sides none disagrees; the whole divergence is one-sided and so outside what the contract guard compares (`OQ-649`). The contract promises 17 paths the model does not carry — including `slug`, `description`, `created_by`, `updated_at`, `archived_at`, `currency`, `labels`, `parent_id` and `schema_version` — and the model carries 5 the contract does not. Three are the same concept in a different shape: the model holds a scalar `derived_from`, `period_from`/`period_to` and `source_fingerprint` where the contract declares objects carrying provenance the scalars cannot express. The recommendation is to split them — the flat fields are the model catching up to a specification that is ahead of it, the three structural ones are a real shape question about a Phase-1a artifact. Trimming the contract to match the code is rejected outright as deleting the specification to make the tooling agree. Options are in [`../open-questions.md`](open-questions.md). **Dispositioned, not delivered** against FR-451. Owner: maintainer. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (c) — 14 flat fields adopt the contract's scalars; derived_from, period_* and source_fingerprint adopt the contract's object forms** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-569 | open question |  | ~~**`pii_class` cannot express "nobody has classified this column", so the platform's two different silences are both stored and displayed as the affirmative statement `none`.** Raised 2026-08-24 (WK-664); every measurement taken at `e2ae7c6` and re-checked at `1c80638`, between which no cited file moved. `PiiClass` has five members and `DataDictionaryEntry.pii_class` defaults to `PiiClass.NONE` (`packages/model-schema/src/model_schema/datasets.py:96-115,142-143`), while the field immediately above it, `semantic_type`, is optional. The dictionary is authored, not inferred — its own docstring says so at `:131-135` — so an unauthored field has no observation behind it. **Two distinct silences exist and neither is visible.** *(i) No dictionary entry for the column at all* — 21 of 23 `create_dataset` call sites omit `data_dictionary` entirely, including `backend/tests/test_wf01_journey.py:189`, the dataset carrying the Phase 1b exit criterion. *(ii) An entry exists but was never classified* — `examples/fremtpl2/seed.py:132`'s `DICTIONARY` describes 13 columns and sets `pii_class` on 1; the 12 silent ones include `driv_age`, `area`, `density` and `region`, age plus geography, the textbook quasi-identifier combination, all defaulting to the word `none`. The specification's own canonical example does the same thing (`01-data-management.md:231-234`): four columns, one classified, `driver_age` left silent eleven lines above the sentence that enumerates what `pii_class` may be. **The default is not merely displayed, it is persisted as a positive assertion**: `_dictionary_json` (`backend/src/app/platform/datasets.py:219-220`) is `model_dump(mode="json")` with no `exclude_unset`, so every stored entry carries `"pii_class": "none"` whether or not a human typed it, and the audit chain records that same post-default value in both `before` and `after` (`:294-295`) — the distinction is destroyed at write time, for every row already in every database. Downstream the UI prints it as a considered answer: `frontend/src/views/DatasetDetailView.vue:352` renders `pii_class ?? "none"` beside `:323`'s honest `semantic_type ?? "—"`, same table, same row, opposite conventions. Silence (i) is worse than mislabelled, it is invisible: `:29` derives the displayed column list from `Object.keys(data_dictionary)`, so a column the dictionary never mentions never appears. **What makes this a design question and not a bug is that no requirement is broken today** — FR-39, FR-40 and FR-12 are all phrased on the positive class, "columns *classified*/*tagged* `direct_identifier`", so an unclassified column is outside their scope by their own wording and `_refuse_direct_identifiers` correctly does not fire. This is `CLAUDE.md` §0's "a capability not yet specified", not its "code and spec disagree". **The clearest statement of the gap is a green test the repository already ships**: `backend/tests/test_ingestion.py:468`, marked for FR-40, uploads a CSV whose `customer_email` column holds an address against a dataset built with an empty dictionary and asserts the rows are written. It is a deliberate negative control and it does prove the check does not refuse everything — but its name is `test_a_column_the_dictionary_does_not_forbid_is_not_refused`, and the fixture supplies a dictionary that forbids nothing **because it says nothing**. The suite's own negative control cannot tell the two silences apart either. *(amended 2026-08-24, the day this row was raised, in two directions — one correcting this row, one recording a change to the artifact it cites.* ***First, this row credited the control with more than it proved.*** *"It does prove the check does not refuse everything" is false, and the control's own docstring made the same claim. Mutating the per-column filter to `present = [c for c in columns]` — refuse every column — left it green, because with no dictionary `forbidden` is empty and the refusal returns before reaching that line. A second mutation, `forbidden = set(dataset.data_dictionary)` — refuse every* ***classified*** *column, which is precisely the confusion between "classified and permitted" and "never classified" that this row is about — left the* ***entire module*** *green at 17 passed.* ***Second, the control has since been strengthened*** *so that its fixture classifies `customer_email` `quasi_identifier`: in the dictionary and permitted. It now fails on the second mutation by its own predicate. The illustration this row drew from it therefore no longer stands at HEAD, and is kept above as the finding's provenance rather than as a current reading of the file. The question is untouched: strengthening the control moved it off silence (ii) onto a classified column, which is why it can now do its job — the platform still cannot express silence (i) or (ii), and no requirement yet makes either consequential.)*~~ | **Read first: a type change is inert on its own.** `modelling_forbidden_columns` (`datasets.py:195-205`) tests `entry.pii_class in MODELLING_FORBIDDEN_PII`, and `None` is absent from that frozenset exactly as `PiiClass.NONE` is; its sole caller `_refuse_direct_identifiers` (`ingestion.py:498-500`) early-returns on an empty result. Every option below makes the silences *distinguishable*; **none makes either one *consequential* unless (e) is decided with it.** A requirement that only widens a type specifies nothing. **(a) Nothing.** Free. Keeps a governed classification whose unset state is stored and shown to an actuary as a decision, in the one screen `01` §5.3 provides for reading it. **(b) A sixth member, `PiiClass.UNCLASSIFIED`.** The house pattern for "no verdict here" is an explicit member on a *required* field, never optional: `RuleOutcome` (`validation.py:64-69`) spells both non-verdict states, `ERROR` and `SKIPPED`, as members; `RuleResult.outcome` is required with no default; and `counts` (`:502-506`) is built from `dict.fromkeys(...)`, so each non-verdict state is positively enumerated rather than inferred from an absence. Contract cost is narrow and known — one string in the flat enum at `docs/contracts/openapi/generated.json:7837-7847` and one property at `:2171-2173`, with no hand-authored contract tier touched and the generated TS field staying required and non-nullable. **Its real cost is the migration**, and the repository has ruled on that shape twice, both times the same way (`backend/migrations/versions/d4e5f6a7b8c9_modelling_diagnostics.py:18-22`, which demotes to `draft` rather than invent an artifact, and `82edffbe1dce_dataset_owner.py:57-60`, which refuses rather than invent an owner). Because `_dictionary_json` already flattened authored and defaulted rows into the same bytes, **no migration can tell existing rows apart**: the honest choices are to relabel every stored `none` as `unclassified` — accurate for the great majority, wrong wherever someone did type it — or to refuse, and neither is free. **(c) Make the field optional** (`PiiClass \| None`). Matches no pattern in `model_schema`. The neighbour that looks like precedent is not one: `DataDictionaryEntry.semantic_type` has **zero runtime consumers** — every consuming site is `ColumnProfile.semantic_type`, which is required and machine-inferred — so its nullability is evidence that nobody had to decide, not that this was decided. It also **recreates the defect one level down**: in JSONB a stored `null` and an absent key both deserialise to `None`, so null-versus-absent replaces unset-versus-`none`. Contract cost is strictly worse than (b) — the field widens from a flat enum to a nullable union and the generated TS field stops being required. **(d) A derived `unclassified_columns`, beside the existing `modelling_forbidden_columns`.** The repository has already answered this exact concern in this exact shape: `ValidationRuleSet.empty_layers` (`validation.py:166-184`, FR-45) is a contract-visible positive enumeration of *what was not covered*, promoted to a computed field precisely because "silence would let a rule set lose its whole distributional layer in an edit and look complete." Alone among the options it reaches **both** silences, because it is computed against the Version's column list rather than against the dictionary's own keys. No wire-format change, no enum edit, and **no migration** — it is a projection of state already stored, so it is correct on historical rows that (b) and (c) cannot repair. Its cost is that it needs the Version's schema, which is where the scoping question bites. **(e) The behavioural half — orthogonal, and decided with whichever of (b)–(d) is chosen.** Does an unclassified column (i) block the `draft → validated` transition under FR-46, (ii) warn on the report and in the UI, or (iii) refuse only to be used as a Factor? **The precedent usually cited for (i) settles less than it appears to.** FR-48's "an unrun rule is never treated as a pass" is scoped to `error`; the glossary at `01-data-management.md:64` separates `error` ("the rule itself blew up") from `skipped` ("a dependency was unavailable"), and `ValidationReport.overall` (`validation.py:541-548`) branches on `FAIL`, `ERROR` and `WARN` and falls through to `PASS` with **no `SKIPPED` branch** — an all-skipped report passes, and `frontend/src/api/validation.ts:57-63` bands `skipped` with `pass`. An unauthored classification is the `skipped` shape, not the `error` shape; followed faithfully the precedent licenses "name the absence and enumerate it" and licenses nothing about blocking. **(f) One cost every option except (a) incurs, and one that no option touches.** `01-data-management.md:241` states the five values as an exhaustive set, so it is amended in the same commit and `generated.json` regenerates under FR-451's drift gate. Separately, `frontend/src/api/datasets.ts:79` reads `entry?.pii_class ? MODELLING_FORBIDDEN.includes(entry.pii_class) : false` — **a falsy class fails open**, returning "safe to model" with no type error anywhere, which is exactly what a null would do under (c). That line is a required edit under every option and **no schema change reaches it**; the same file hand-copies `MODELLING_FORBIDDEN` from `datasets.py:113-115`, a shape duplicated against `CLAUDE.md` §2 and worth deriving whatever is decided here. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (d) with (e)(ii) — a derived unclassified_columns, warn and name, do not block; (b) preferred over (c) if stored state is wanted** |  |  |  |
| OQ-569 | open question |  | ~~**`pii_class` cannot express "nobody has classified this column", so the platform's two different silences are both stored and displayed as the affirmative statement `none`.**~~ Raised 2026-08-24 (WK-664). `PiiClass` has five members and `DataDictionaryEntry.pii_class` defaults to `PiiClass.NONE`, while the `semantic_type` field immediately above it is optional; the dictionary is authored rather than inferred, so an unauthored field has no observation behind it. Two silences exist and neither is visible: **(i)** the column has no dictionary entry at all — 21 of 23 `create_dataset` call sites omit `data_dictionary`, including the dataset carrying the Phase 1b exit criterion — and **(ii)** an entry exists but was never classified — `examples/fremtpl2/seed.py`'s `DICTIONARY` describes 13 columns and classifies 1, leaving `driv_age`, `area`, `density` and `region` defaulting to the word `none`. §4's own canonical example at :231-234 does the same thing eleven lines above the sentence that enumerates what `pii_class` may be. The default is **persisted as a positive assertion** — `_dictionary_json` dumps without `exclude_unset`, so the authored/defaulted distinction is destroyed at write time for every row already stored, and the audit chain records the post-default value on both sides. §5.3's dataset detail then renders it as a considered answer beside an honest em-dash for `semantic_type`, and silence (i) is not merely mislabelled but invisible, since the displayed column list is derived from the dictionary's own keys. **No requirement is broken today**: FR-39 and FR-40 are phrased on the positive class, so an unclassified column is outside their scope by their own wording — this is `CLAUDE.md` §0's "a capability not yet specified". Options run from doing nothing, through a sixth `unclassified` enum member or an optional field, to a derived `unclassified_columns` projection in the shape FR-45's `empty_layers` already established; a separate and orthogonal question is whether an unclassified column blocks `draft → validated` under FR-46, warns, or only refuses Factor use. The recommendation is the derived projection plus a warning, on the grounds that only it reaches silence (i) and only it is correct about rows already written, and that the nearest precedent is `update_dictionary` being a replace and not a merge — "a merge would make a removal indistinguishable from an omission" — which is this question decided at the entry level and left open at the field level. Note that :241 states the five values as an exhaustive set and is amended by every option but the first. Options are in [`../open-questions.md`](open-questions.md). Owner: unowned. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (d) with (e)(ii) — a derived unclassified_columns, warn and name, do not block; (b) preferred over (c) if stored state is wanted** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-570 | open question |  | ~~**A validation layer that is present but could not run is indistinguishable from one that passed, and the field written to prevent exactly that cannot see it.** Raised 2026-08-25 (WK-664 docs backlog), while writing FR-57. When a Rule Set enables distributional rules but pins no Reference Dataset Version, each of those rules returns `skipped` with the reason "no reference version to compare against" (`packages/pricing-core/src/pricing_core/data/validate.py`), which `_run_one` records as `RuleOutcome.SKIPPED`. `ValidationReport.overall` (`packages/model-schema/src/model_schema/validation.py`) branches on `FAIL`, `ERROR` and `WARN` and falls through to `PASS` — so a report whose **entire** distributional layer never ran reads `pass`, `permits_validation` is `True`, and the Dataset Version is promotable on the strength of checks that did not happen. **`empty_layers` does not catch it.** It is `ALL_LAYERS - covered_layers`, and `covered_layers` is taken from the rule set's *enabled* entries, so a layer that is enabled and unrunnable counts as covered. Its own docstring states why it exists — "silence would let a rule set lose its whole distributional layer in an edit and look complete" — which describes this case exactly, measured by the very field written to refuse it. The gap is that FR-45's completeness test is **rule-set-side**, asking what the set declares, and no **report-side** analogue asks what the run produced. There is no write-path guard either: `replace_rule_set` stores `reference_dataset_version_id` without checking it against the layers its members belong to. **No requirement is broken today.** FR-48's "an unrun rule is never treated as a pass" is scoped to `error`, and §2's glossary separates `error` ("the rule itself blew up") from `skipped` ("a dependency was unavailable") — an absent reference is squarely the second. This is `CLAUDE.md` §0's "a capability not yet specified", not its "code and spec disagree". *The mechanism is already written down in this document, inside `OQ-569`, where the `PASS` fallthrough is cited as licence for that row's own recommendation about `pii_class`. It has never been raised as a question in its own right; that is what this row does, and nothing here reopens `OQ-569`.*~~ | **(a) Nothing.** Free, and defensible on the narrow ground that `skipped` is an honest per-rule verdict already present in `results`. The cost is that the honesty stops at the rule: the layer, the overall verdict and the promotion decision all read as though the checks ran, and an actuary reading a `pass` badge cannot learn that PSI was never computed without expanding every distributional rule individually. **(b) A report-side `unrun_layers`,** computed on `ValidationReport` in the shape `empty_layers` already established — the layers for which every result is `SKIPPED`. Contract-visible, so the screen can name the layer that did not run beside the badge that says `pass`. No wire-format break and **no migration**, because it is a projection of results already stored, which makes it correct about reports written months ago in a way no enum change can be. **(c) A `SKIPPED` branch in `overall`** — either a fifth `OverallOutcome` or banding skipped with `PASS_WITH_WARNINGS`. It reaches the badge with no client change, but it re-grades every report already written, and the frontend bands `skipped` with `pass` deliberately (`frontend/src/api/validation.ts`), so the edit is not confined to one field. It also overstates the common case: one skipped rule among thirty-eight is not a warning about the run. **(d) Refuse at write time** — reject a Rule Set that enables a distributional rule with no reference pinned. Cheap, and it prevents the case rather than reporting it. But it over-refuses, because a Rule Set is routinely authored before the data it will run against exists, and FR-57 keeps the pin optional on exactly that ground; and it reaches only this one cause, when a rule can be skipped for others. **(e) The orthogonal half, answered together with whichever of (b) to (d) is chosen:** does an unrun layer *block* `draft → validated` under FR-46, or only warn on the report and in the UI? **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (b), with (e) warn-and-name, do not block; (d) rejected** |  |  |  |
| OQ-570 | open question |  | ~~**A validation layer that is present but could not run is indistinguishable from one that passed, and the field written to prevent exactly that cannot see it.**~~ Raised 2026-08-25 (WK-664 docs backlog), while writing FR-57. A Rule Set that enables distributional rules but pins no Reference Dataset Version makes every one of them return `skipped`; `ValidationReport.overall` branches on `FAIL`, `ERROR` and `WARN` and falls through to `PASS`, so a report whose entire distributional layer never ran reads `pass` and `permits_validation` is `True` — the Dataset Version is promotable on checks that did not happen. `empty_layers` cannot catch it, because `covered_layers` is taken from the rule set's *enabled* entries, so an enabled-but-unrunnable layer counts as covered; the docstring's own justification, that silence would let a rule set "lose its whole distributional layer in an edit and look complete", describes this case measured by the very field written to refuse it. FR-45's completeness test is rule-set-side and no report-side analogue exists, and `replace_rule_set` adds no write-path guard. No requirement is broken: FR-48's "an unrun rule is never treated as a pass" is scoped to `error`, which §2's glossary separates from `skipped`, so this is `CLAUDE.md` §0's "a capability not yet specified". Options run from doing nothing, through a derived report-side `unrun_layers` in the shape `empty_layers` already established, to a `SKIPPED` branch in `overall` or a write-time refusal; a separate and orthogonal question is whether an unrun layer blocks `draft → validated` under FR-46 or only warns. The recommendation is the derived projection plus a warning, on the grounds that it needs no migration and is therefore correct about reports already written, that blocking is licensed only by reading FR-48 across a line the glossary draws, and that a write-time refusal fixes one cause where the condition is what governs promotion. Note that this mechanism is already cited inside `OQ-569` as licence for that row's recommendation, and nothing here reopens it. Options are in [`../open-questions.md`](open-questions.md). Owner: unowned. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (b), with (e) warn-and-name, do not block; (d) rejected** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-571 | open question |  | ~~**`spline`, `polynomial` and `offset` Factors are refused by name with no owner and no schedule. Which of the three, if any, does the platform commit to — and when?** FR-208 contains them correctly (a refusal beats a raw column silently substituted) but containment is not a plan, and WK-661 closes with three arms of FR-83's closed set counted among the evidenced because a test marks the refusal.~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-22: none of the three is committed to a slice, and they no longer share a verdict.** `offset` superseded — FR-209. `spline` and `polynomial` stay declared and refused, gated on FR-210, owner WK-690. | **(a) Commit to all three in Phase 2, one slice.** Each needs its own `Factor` contract field and a `resolve_factors` argument, so they share a shape and splitting them triples the contract churn. `offset` is the odd one — an offset is already expressible on a `GlmSpec` (FR-116), so an `offset` *Factor* may be a duplicate mechanism rather than a missing one, and the slice would have to say which. **(b) Commit to `spline` only, supersede `polynomial` and `offset`.** A spline is what a UK pricing actuary actually asks for on a continuous rating variable; a polynomial is a worse answer to the same question, and `offset` is FR-116's job. Smallest honest scope, at the cost of narrowing a set FR-83 declared closed in Phase 0. **(c) Supersede all three and narrow FR-83's set to five.** The refusals become permanent and the enum shrinks to what the platform means. Cheapest, and it forecloses a capability on no evidence about demand. **(d) Leave as is.** Rejected on its face: this is the silence §13 rule 1 forbids, and naming it here is the point of the question. |  |  |  |
| OQ-571 | open question |  | ~~**`spline`, `polynomial` and `offset` Factors are refused by name with no owner and no schedule. Which of the three, if any, does the platform commit to — and when?** FR-208 contains them correctly (a refusal beats a raw column silently substituted) but containment is not a plan, and WK-661 closes with three arms of FR-83's closed set counted among the evidenced because a test marks the refusal. Raised 2026-08-22 (WK-661, the audit-remediation slice).~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-22: none of the three is committed to a slice, and they no longer share a verdict.** `offset` is superseded as a Factor type, the arm kept in the published contract and its refusal made permanent — FR-209. `spline` and `polynomial` stay declared and stay refused, gated on FR-210 and owned by WK-690, because the blocker is neither of them: no continuous Factor can be rated or reviewed today, including the `identity`-over-numeric one that already resolves. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-572 | open question |  | ~~**NFR-479's "diagnostics ≤ 30 % of fit wall-clock" and FR-172's type-III likelihood-ratio tests cannot both hold as written. Which moves?** Measured 2026-08-22: diagnostics cost **510 %** of fit wall-clock at 12 factors and **1 388 %** at 24, and **3 002 %** on the GBM path. Everything `compute_diagnostics` does *except* type-III fits the budget, at 9.0–9.5 %. The arithmetic is not a slow function: type-III **drops each factor and refits**, so a diagnostic containing *F* refits of the model cannot cost 30 % of one fit for any *F* above zero — and FR-170 requires diagnostics computed once at fit time, so there is no later pass to move them to. A second question rides along: **which denominator the budget means**, since `fit_seconds` (the solve) excludes the factor resolution and design-matrix construction the caller also waits for, and the no-type-III figure is 32.1 % against wall-clock but 55.2 % against `fit_seconds`.~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-22: option (a), denominator fit wall-clock.** NFR-479 re-scoped and re-set to 50 % at a named scale; type-III becomes NFR-487, the GBM path NFR-488. | **(a) Scope NFR-479 to diagnostics excluding type-III, and give the type-III block its own budget.** Honest about what the 30 % was ever measuring — it fits everything else at 9.0–9.5 % — and keeps FR-170's compute-once property intact. Costs a second number to maintain, and a reader must know both. **(b) Make type-III a separate Job the caller asks for.** The fit returns quickly, significance arrives on request. Costs FR-170's "computed once at fit time, read thereafter" for this one block, which is a real weakening: a diagnostic a caller has to remember to ask for is one a model document can be assembled without. **(c) Replace the refit with a score or Wald approximation.** One fit total, budget met as written. But it answers a *slightly different question* — and FR-172 chose the likelihood-ratio test deliberately, so this is a change to what the platform reports, not to how fast it reports it. **(d) Raise the budget to fit the measurement.** Rejected on its face: a budget rewritten to whatever was measured is not a budget, and NFR-479 exists to stop diagnostics quietly dominating a fit. |  |  |  |
| OQ-572 | open question |  | ~~**NFR-479's "diagnostics ≤ 30 % of fit wall-clock" and FR-172's type-III likelihood-ratio tests cannot both hold as written. Which moves?** Measured 2026-08-22: diagnostics cost **510 %** of fit wall-clock at 12 factors and **1 388 %** at 24, and **3 002 %** on the GBM path. Everything `compute_diagnostics` does *except* type-III fits the budget, at 9.0–9.5 %. The arithmetic is not a slow function: type-III **drops each factor and refits**, so a diagnostic containing *F* refits of the model cannot cost 30 % of one fit for any *F* above zero — and FR-170 requires diagnostics computed once at fit time, so there is no later pass to move them to. A second question rides along: **which denominator the budget means**, since `fit_seconds` (the solve) excludes the factor resolution and design-matrix construction the caller also waits for, and the no-type-III figure is 32.1 % against wall-clock but 55.2 % against `fit_seconds`. Raised 2026-08-22 (WK-661, the audit-remediation slice), from the first measurement of §9's NFR-479.~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-22: option (a), denominator settled as fit wall-clock.** NFR-479 is re-scoped to exclude the per-factor block and re-set to 50 % at a named scale; the type-III block becomes NFR-487, one fit wall-clock per *tested* factor; the GBM path becomes NFR-488, priced per scoring pass rather than per factor because the driver is grid points. The uncapped categorical sweep it exposed is OQ-596. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-573 | open question |  | ~~Ship `expression` custom objectives in Phase 1, or templates only?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-15: templates in Phase 1, expressions in Phase 2, certification machinery built in Phase 1 for both.** | Templates only: the §4.5 catalogue already covers Tweedie tuning, capping, and asymmetric losses — i.e. most of the stated need — with zero parser/sandbox risk. Expressions in Phase 1: the headline differentiator lands early, but the AST parser, SymPy derivation, and certification are all new and all security-relevant at once. |  |  |  |
| OQ-573 | open question |  | ~~Should `expression` custom objectives ship in Phase 1 at all, or only the `template` catalogue (§4.5)?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-15: templates in Phase 1, expressions in Phase 2 — and the certification machinery is built in Phase 1 regardless.** Specified as FR-150 and FR-151. The restricted parser is not the deferred part and never was: it already exists for `01` FR-36. What Phase 2 adds is symbolic derivation, a second compilation target, and the review path for a loss a user wrote. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-574 | open question |  | ~~GBM prediction intervals — how?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-15: `uncertainty: unavailable` with a typed reason by default; opt-in paired quantile models; the variance approximation is never shipped.** | Paired quantile models: honest, 2–3× fit cost, and the quantiles can cross. Variance-model approximation: cheap, easy to misread as a genuine predictive interval. `uncertainty: unavailable`: honest and free, but violates the spirit of R5 for GBMs specifically. |  |  |  |
| OQ-574 | open question |  | ~~GBM prediction intervals: paired quantile models, "uncertainty unavailable", or a variance-model approximation?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-15: `uncertainty: unavailable` with a typed reason by default, opt-in paired quantile models, and the variance approximation is never shipped.** Specified as FR-198 and FR-199. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-575 | open question |  | ~~Can a Rating Version rate on the GLM approximation instead of calling the GBM?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-17: yes — both modes, and the mode is the Rating Version's rather than the step's.** | Rate on the approximation: fully tabular, inspectable, fast, and it is what most insurers actually deploy — but the deployed prices are then *not* the model that was validated. Call the GBM: exact, but the rating structure contains a black box on the p99 latency path. |  |  |  |
| OQ-575 | open question |  | ~~Is the GLM approximation of a GBM a *transparency artifact* only, or should it be directly rateable — i.e. can a Rating Version rate on the approximation instead of calling the GBM, trading fidelity for a fully tabular rating structure?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-17: both modes, and the mode belongs to the Rating Version rather than to the step.** Specified as `03` FR-223, which pins every `model_call` step's `mode` to `RatingVersion.model_reference_mode` and refuses a disagreement at save time; `03` FR-222 was ahead of its question rather than wrong and stands as written. What an approximation must *prove* before deploying in that mode is carved out as **OQ-576**, and **OQ-577** is unblocked. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-576 | open question |  | ~~What must a GLM approximation prove before a Rating Version may **deploy** in `approximation` mode?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-18: a dislocation run against the same version in `exact` mode, inside a workspace-declared premium-deviation threshold, with the fidelity statement as the pre-check.** | Carved out of OQ-575 when it was decided, 2026-08-17. FR-136's fidelity statement is descriptive — it is computed, stored and shown, and nothing refuses anything on it — so a mode whose deployed prices are, by construction, not the model that was validated currently has no floor. **A numeric floor on the artifact:** a workspace-declared minimum R² and maximum absolute relativity deviation, checked at the Rating Version's approval beside the other evidence (`03` FR-257). Cheap, and refuses the mode where the surrogate is plainly poor — but any single threshold is arbitrary, and coefficient agreement is not premium agreement. **A dislocation-based gate:** run `03` FR-263's existing dislocation report with `exact` as baseline and `approximation` as candidate, and threshold *that*. It measures the quantity that matters, on the actual book — but costs a portfolio run per approval and requires `exact` mode to be runnable at approval time. **Nothing beyond the statement:** the approver reads the fidelity numbers and decides, which is how every other piece of evidence is treated. |  |  |  |
| OQ-576 | open question |  | ~~What must a GLM approximation prove before a Rating Version may **deploy** in `approximation` mode?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-18: a dislocation run against the same version in `exact` mode, within a workspace-declared premium-deviation threshold, with FR-136's fidelity statement kept as the cheap pre-check — `03` FR-224**, Phase 2, with the deployment path it gates. R² answers a question about coefficients; the approval question is how different the prices will be. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-577 | open question |  | ~~Should the GLM approximation of a GBM (FR-133) be persisted as a **Model** in its own right, or carried inside the transparency artifact?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-18: as a Model, owned by Phase 1b.** **Built 2026-08-19 (WK-661).** | Raised by building it, 2026-08-17. §4.9's `approximating_model_id` implies the first and the field is `None` in the built artifact. **As a Model:** it becomes citable, versioned and diffable like any other, `02` §4.11's comparison could put a GBM beside its own approximation, and OQ-575's question — can a Rating Version rate on the approximation — has an obvious referent if it is ever answered yes. **Inside the artifact:** a Model needs a spec, and this one's response column is *another model's prediction* rather than a dataset column, so `dataset_version_id`, `spec_hash` and R1 all mean something slightly different; it would also need diagnostics of its own to reach `fitted` (§4.8), which are diagnostics of a surrogate against a surrogate target. |  |  |  |
| OQ-577 | open question |  | ~~Is the GLM approximation of a GBM (FR-133) a **Model** in its own right, or a block inside the transparency artifact?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-18: a Model in its own right — FR-137**, owned by Phase 1b and due before anything references a transparency artifact by identifier. **Built 2026-08-19 (WK-661).** The deciding argument was supplied by OQ-575's answer rather than by this row: an `approximation`-mode Rating Version pins what it rates on, and FR-20 requires a pin to resolve to an artifact with a status, which a `TransparencyArtifact` does not have. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-578 | open question |  | ~~Auto-detect interaction candidates from SHAP interaction values?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-15: surfaced as suggestions with evidence, never auto-added.** | Auto-detection surfaces genuine structure an actuary would miss, and is a well-worn path to overfitting and to rating structures nobody can defend. |  |  |  |
| OQ-578 | open question |  | ~~Interactions as explicit Factors only, or also automatically-detected candidates from SHAP interaction values?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-15: detected candidates are surfaced as suggestions with their exposure share and holdout lift; only an explicit Factor with a rationale can enter a Model Spec.** Specified as FR-135. *Amended 2026-08-23: the two named numbers are withdrawn — a per-pair exposure share is `1.0` by construction and holdout lift was never defined anywhere in the suite — and what replaces them is OQ-601, decided the same day: a holdout strength ratio (FR-168). The decision itself stands: suggestions, never additions.* |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-579 | open question |  | ~~Which credibility standard for `credibility_weighted` grouping?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-15: both — limited fluctuation as the default, Bühlmann–Straub selectable, the choice recorded per grouping.** | Limited fluctuation (classical): simple, familiar to UK GI actuaries, one parameter (standard for full credibility), theoretically weak. Bühlmann–Straub: defensible, handles heterogeneous exposure properly, needs variance components that are awkward to estimate on thin cells and harder to explain in a model document. |  |  |  |
| OQ-579 | open question |  | ~~Which credibility standard for `credibility_weighted` grouping — limited fluctuation or Bühlmann–Straub?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-15: both, limited fluctuation as the default, recorded per grouping.** Specified as FR-106, with `credibility_model`, its `(p, k)` pair and Bühlmann–Straub's variance components persisted in §4.3. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-580 | open question |  | ~~Hard gate on factor count / exposure-per-parameter, or diagnostic warning only?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-15: a diagnostic always; a gate only where a workspace configures one, unset by default.** | Hard gate: prevents a class of overfitted models reaching approval, and will block legitimate large models on large books. Warning: relies on the Approver noticing. |  |  |  |
| OQ-580 | open question |  | ~~Hard gate on factor count / exposure-per-parameter, or a diagnostic warning?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-15: a diagnostic always, and a gate only where a workspace configures one — unset by default.** Specified as FR-185; the judgement belongs to the Approver (`06`), not to a constant chosen here. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-581 | open question |  | ~~How are protected-characteristic proxies detected, and what happens when detection fires?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-15: the `prohibited` flag is the whole of Phases 1–2; a Phase 3 proxy assessment produces evidence, never a block.** | Needs (a) a statistical test — e.g. mutual information or AUC of the factor predicting a protected characteristic on a reference population — and (b) a policy. We do not hold protected characteristics, so any test needs an external reference dataset the insurer supplies. |  |  |  |
| OQ-581 | open question |  | ~~How are protected-characteristic proxies detected, and what happens when detection fires?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-15: the `prohibited` flag is the whole of Phases 1–2; a Phase 3 proxy assessment produces evidence for the approval request and never a block.** Specified as FR-91; delivery is a Phase 3 deliverable, so the decision is made and the work is not now. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-582 | open question |  | ~~Does the GLM spine grow to meet `02` §4's field sets, or does §4 narrow to a staged contract?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-17: §4 is a staged contract, re-widened as each slice lands.** | Found by auditing the spine, 2026-08-15. §4.1 declares `banding_id`, `grouping_id` and `expression` on `Factor`; §4.4 declares `split_ref`, `filter`, `loss_treatment` and a nested `regularisation` block on `ModelSpec`; §4.8 declares `diagnostics_id`, `transparency_artifact_id`, `custom_objective_ref` and `approval_request_id` on `Model`, plus a `status ≥ fitted ⟹ diagnostics_id set` invariant the spine cannot meet because diagnostics do not exist yet. The spine implements a subset and enforces a different invariant (`fitted ⟹ fit_result`). **Grow the code**: the contract is the design and the frontend generates from it, so a field declared and unpopulated is at least visible. **Narrow the spec**: a contract carrying eleven always-null fields teaches a reader that null means nothing rather than not-yet, and `loss_treatment` in particular changes `spec_hash` for every stored model when it arrives. |  |  |  |
| OQ-582 | open question |  | ~~Does the GLM spine grow to meet §4's field sets, or does §4 narrow to a staged contract? §4.1, §4.4 and §4.8 declare fields the spine does not implement, and §4.8's `status ≥ fitted ⟹ diagnostics_id set` cannot be met while diagnostics do not exist.~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-17: §4 is a staged contract, re-widened as each slice lands — decided by demonstration, after the pattern ran six times.** Specified as FR-207, which names every remaining residual with a verdict and an owner; the standing `spec_hash` versioning rule the question set as its own precondition is now FR-206; `expression`'s verdict is FR-208 and §4.8's R3 is restated enforceably by FR-211. §4.4's nested `regularisation` block was a divergence rather than a staged field and is corrected in place. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-583 | open question |  | ~~Do the `tree` banding (FR-98) and `tree` grouping (FR-105) methods justify adding a tree learner to `pricing-core`'s declared dependencies?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-17: declare `scikit-learn` and implement both with `DecisionTreeRegressor`.** | Raised by implementing the other seven methods, 2026-08-15. **Add the dependency:** both methods are declared in the spec, both are what an actuary reaches for when the response is what should decide the cut, and scikit-learn is already present transitively through `glum` — so the cost is a line in `pyproject.toml` and a row in `skills-map.md`, not a new subsystem. **Leave them refused:** `pricing-core`'s dependency list is the thing ADR-703 makes reviewable, a transitive dependency is not a declared one, and seven of nine methods already ship. What is *not* an option is the third path taken by neither: returning quantile boundaries under the label `tree`, which records a method as one it is not. |  |  |  |
| OQ-583 | open question |  | ~~Do `tree` banding boundaries (FR-98) and `tree` grouping (FR-105) justify adding a tree learner to `pricing-core`'s dependencies?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-17: declare `scikit-learn` and fit a CART tree in both — the label `tree` must name the instrument.** Specified as FR-103, with the dependency in §8; a one-tree booster would have cost no dependency at all and was rejected because its splits are not CART's. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-584 | open question |  | ~~May an `interaction` Factor cross a **continuous** operand?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-18: no — refused by name, and no product term at any intent.** | Raised 2026-08-18 (WK-661, the interaction slice) by building the crossing and finding the choice unavoidable at the first continuous operand. **Refuse it, and say so** — what this slice implements: the refusal names the operand and the remedy (band or group it, then cross the result), so the actuary gets a rateable structure by construction. **Allow a product term** — legitimate GLM practice and the thing an analyst reaches for when the effect really is a smooth slope; but `03`'s rating DAG is a graph of *tables*, and a varying slope has no cell, so the model would fit and could not be deployed as a rate table — the failure arriving at the rating slice rather than at the factor. **Allow it only for `intent: diagnostic` factors** — the narrow version: a slope is fine for something never rated on, and FR-88 already distinguishes intents. |  |  |  |
| OQ-584 | open question |  | ~~May an `interaction` Factor cross a **continuous** operand?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-18: no — refused by name, and no product term at any intent — FR-93**, which ratifies what the interaction slice built and names the `diagnostic`-intent variant as the likely eventual answer, to be decided against a rate table that exists. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-585 | open question |  | ~~Should the platform ever offer a **true prediction interval** — one carrying the process variance `φ·V(μ)` — and if so, under what name?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-18: not until a named consumer asks; then `prediction_interval`, aggregate first.** | Raised 2026-08-18 (WK-661, the prediction slice) on discovering the requirement named a quantity its stated mechanism cannot produce. **Never offer one** — what this slice implements: for a frequency model on a single policy the process variance dominates so completely that the honest interval is very nearly "0 or 1 claims", which is true and prices nothing, and a reader who asked for uncertainty and got that will read it as a malfunction. **Offer it as a second, separately-named kind** — `prediction_interval` beside `confidence_interval_mean`, computed as `φ·V(μ)` from the stored dispersion; cheap, correct, and defensible for severity and burning-cost models where the interval is genuinely informative. **Offer it only for aggregate predictions** — over a portfolio the process variance averages away and a predictive interval on total expected claims is both meaningful and what a capital or reserving reader actually wants. |  |  |  |
| OQ-585 | open question |  | ~~Should the platform ever offer a **true prediction interval** — one carrying the process variance `φ·V(μ)` — and under what name?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-18: not until a named consumer asks — FR-196**, which fixes the trigger (the first aggregate consumer), the name (`prediction_interval`) and the shape (aggregate first, and `confidence_interval_mean` never silently widened). |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-586 | open question |  | ~~A **penalised** GLM (`alpha > 0`) reports standard errors and now a confidence interval computed from a covariance matrix `glum` itself warns is incorrect. What should a penalised fit report as its uncertainty?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-18: report both, and state the basis they were computed on.** | Raised 2026-08-18 (WK-661, the prediction slice) from the warning `glum` emits on every penalised fit in the suite; `alpha` defaults to `0.0` so the default path is unaffected, and `02` §4.8's comparison journey fits at `alpha = 25.0` deliberately. **Report it anyway, as now** — the sandwich-free matrix is the unpenalised information matrix, which understates the shrinkage the penalty applied and so gives intervals that are too wide for the estimate actually reported; wrong in the conservative direction, but wrong. **Refuse the interval** with a fourth `UnavailableReason` — honest, and it would leave FR-113's standard errors reporting from the same matrix the interval just declined to use, which is incoherent unless both go. **Bootstrap or a penalty-aware sandwich** — correct for both, and 200-odd refits per model is a different cost class from a fit. |  |  |  |
| OQ-586 | open question |  | ~~What uncertainty should a **penalised** GLM (`alpha > 0`) report, given that `glum` warns its covariance matrix "will be incorrect"?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-18: report both, and state the basis — FR-197**, which answers FR-113 and FR-194 together because one matrix produces both, and names the bootstrap as the exact answer with the consumer that triggers it. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-587 | open question |  | ~~`GlmDiagnostic.aliasing` is `tuple[str, ...]` — collinear terms named — while `docs/contracts/schemas/diagnostics.schema.json` declares an array of untyped `object`. Should an aliasing entry be a bare term name, or a record such as `{term, aliased_with, reason}`?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-21: bare term names — FR-173, delivered the same day.** | Found 2026-08-19 by widening `test_generated_and_authored_agree_on_scalar_types` from 6 slugs to 11 while applying `OQ-547` — the divergence had been invisible because `diagnostics` was outside the check's parametrize list. **Neither side is obviously the error**, which is why this is a question and not a fix: the model's comment argues the name is the point ("2 terms aliased" tells a reader something is wrong and not which factor to fix), and an object entry would say strictly more. FR-172 asks only for "a VIF/aliasing report" and does not decide it. Pinned meanwhile by `test_the_diagnostics_divergence_is_exactly_the_known_one`, so a *new* divergence in `diagnostics` still fails. Two adjacent unguarded float channels into a `DecimalStr` field were found by the same audit and are recorded here rather than lost: `ReconcileRequest.tolerance` is a bare `Decimal` (a JSON number is still coerced, then stringified into the job parameters), and `pricing-core`'s `ReconciliationResult` is an unvalidated frozen dataclass. |  |  |  |
| OQ-587 | open question |  | ~~`GlmDiagnostic.aliasing` is `tuple[str, ...]` — collinear terms named — while `docs/contracts/schemas/diagnostics.schema.json` declares an array of untyped `object`. Should an aliasing entry be a bare term name, or a record such as `{term, aliased_with, reason}`? Neither side is obviously wrong — a name is what a reader acts on, an object entry says strictly more, and FR-172 asks only for "a VIF/aliasing report".~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-21: bare term names — FR-173**, delivered the same day (contract corrected, pin deleted); the object form waits for WK-664's diagnostic view. Found 2026-08-19 by widening the contract type comparison. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-588 | open question |  | ~~A paired quantile interval covers `Y`; `UncertaintyKind.confidence_interval_mean` covers `E[Y\|x]`; and FR-196 fixes the platform at exactly one kind, naming `prediction_interval` (`φ·V(μ)`, aggregate-first) as the only future second. What does a quantile-pair response call itself?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-19: a third member, `quantile_pair_interval`.** | Raised 2026-08-19 (WK-661, the paired-quantile slice) on finding FR-199's deliverable has no name in the enum FR-196 closed. **Reuse `confidence_interval_mean`:** no contract change, and it is a lie — the pair covers the outcome, not the mean, and FR-196 says that value is "never silently widened". **Use FR-196's reserved `prediction_interval`:** the right *quantity*, and it collides with a reserved name whose stated computation is `φ·V(μ)` from a GLM's dispersion offered for aggregates first — a client matching the value would get a per-row quantile pair where the requirement promised an aggregate variance interval. **A third member, `quantile_pair_interval`:** names the estimator as well as the quantity, so a reader comparing a GBM bound with a GLM bound can tell they are not the same kind of claim; costs an enum member, which FR-196 correctly calls a contract change. |  |  |  |
| OQ-588 | open question |  | ~~A paired quantile interval covers `Y` while `UncertaintyKind.confidence_interval_mean` covers `E[Y\|x]`, and FR-196 fixes the platform at exactly one kind — what does a quantile-pair response call itself?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-19: a third member, `quantile_pair_interval` — FR-201**, which takes neither existing value and leaves FR-196's reserved `prediction_interval` waiting for the aggregate consumer that triggers it. FR-196 is amended by addendum rather than edited. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-589 | open question |  | ~~On a rebuild (`should_fit=False`), `model.transparency` pays a full GLM fit plus a full type-III diagnostics pass — one refit per factor — for numbers it then discards, because the surrogate Model already exists. Should the Job skip that compute and reuse the surrogate's already-fitted numbers instead?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-21: skip the compute on `should_fit=False` and reuse the stored numbers — FR-138.** | Found 2026-08-19 in the final whole-branch review of FR-137 (fix round), reading `backend/src/app/worker/model_handlers.py`'s `_transparency` handler: `build_glm_approximation` and `compute_diagnostics` run unconditionally, before `model_service.reserve_model` reports whether `should_fit` is `True`, so a rebuild against an unchanged spec pays for a full GLM fit (`glum`) and a full type-III diagnostics sweep — one refit per factor, FR-172 — and then discards both: `store()` only ever persists them when `should_fit` is `True` (FR-204). It is correct, not slow by accident: a comment at the call site notes the fit is deterministic under `spec.seed`, which `approximation_spec` carries over from the GBM, so the discarded numbers agree with the ones the first build already stored. Nobody has costed how large that discard is. **Skip the compute when the surrogate is already fitted, reusing its stored numbers:** `reserve_model` already returns `should_fit`, so the handler could branch on it before fitting and, on `False`, load the existing surrogate's `Diagnostics` and the latest `TransparencyArtifact`'s `glm_approximation` block (`r_squared`, `deviance_explained`, `worst_regions`) instead of recomputing them — cheapest, but only correct because the source Model and the surrogate's spec are both immutable once fitted; it would stop being self-evidently current if that ever changed. **Keep recomputing, for a fresh fidelity measurement every time:** today's behaviour, and the one honest argument for it — FR-136 wants the fidelity statement to describe the model as fitted, and a cached number describes a past build event rather than the model. Costs a full fit and a full type-III pass on every rebuild regardless of whether anything could have changed. **Make it conditional — skip only when nothing that could change the numbers has changed:** correct in principle, but needs its own invariant proving nothing between builds could disagree, which is the kind of condition that erodes quietly. |  |  |  |
| OQ-589 | open question |  | ~~On a rebuild (`should_fit=False`), `model.transparency` pays a full GLM fit plus a full type-III diagnostics pass — one refit per factor — for numbers it then discards, because the surrogate Model already exists; nobody has costed it. Should the Job skip that compute and reuse the surrogate's already-fitted numbers, keep recomputing for a fresh fidelity measurement, or make it conditional?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-21: skip the compute on `should_fit=False` and reuse the stored numbers — FR-138**, owned by Phase 1b, before its measurement of the Job's cost against `07`'s job-latency NFRs. Found 2026-08-19 in the final whole-branch review of FR-137 (fix round). |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-590 | open question |  | ~~A Custom Metric never gets differentiated, so §4.7's `ObjectiveCertificate` battery includes checks — `analytic_vs_numeric_gradient`/`hessian`, `convexity`, `branch_discontinuity` — a metric has no gradient or hessian to run them against. Should a Custom Metric's certificate run the full nine-check battery anyway (each derivative check reporting `not_applicable`), or a reduced, metric-specific set?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-19: a reduced certificate, sharing §4.7's check vocabulary rather than its check list.** | Raised 2026-08-19 in the custom-metrics decision gate, on FR-146's argument extended to metrics: a metric that early-stops a fit changes when boosting halts and therefore changes the model, so it needs evidence before submission the same way an objective does. **Full nine-check battery, unused checks marked `not_applicable`:** one `CheckStatus` enum and one certificate shape for both artifacts, but four of nine checks would read `not_applicable` on every version ever certified — a status that exists to answer "not askable here" is not the same as a check nobody wrote, and a reader learns nothing from a field that is always the same value. **A reduced, metric-only check set (`finiteness`, `direction_holds`, `scale_behaviour`, `smoke_evaluation`), sharing `CheckStatus`, `SamplingSpec`, `CertificateOutcome` and `CertificateResult.outcome_of` with §4.7 rather than restating them:** no `not_applicable` padding, and `direction_holds` is a check an objective certificate has no use for (an objective has no declared direction to hold), so the two lists were never going to be the same list. **No certificate at all**, on the argument that a metric does not change fitted coefficients: rejected outright — early stopping decides *which* round's coefficients are kept, which is exactly the lever FR-146 already treats as consequential. |  |  |  |
| OQ-590 | open question |  | ~~Should a Custom Metric's certificate run §4.7's full nine-check `ObjectiveCertificate` battery (each derivative/convexity check reporting `not_applicable`, since a metric has no gradient or hessian), or a reduced, metric-specific check set?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-19: a reduced certificate — `finiteness`, `direction_holds`, `scale_behaviour`, `smoke_evaluation` — FR-157**, sharing §4.7's `CheckStatus`, `SamplingSpec`, `CertificateOutcome` and `outcome_of` unchanged rather than its check list. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-591 | open question |  | ~~Does a Custom Metric define its own value computation — a metric-specific template catalogue, or its own `expression` grammar mirroring FR-144 — or does it name an existing `ObjectiveTemplate` (§4.5) and reuse that template's loss as its value?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-19: a metric names an `ObjectiveTemplate` and reuses its loss, evaluated as an exposure-weighted mean.** | Raised 2026-08-19 in the custom-metrics decision gate. A metric's *value* is a per-observation loss aggregated over a partition — the same shape an objective's loss already has — so the question is whether that shape gets defined twice. **A metric-specific template catalogue, parallel to §4.5:** guarantees a metric can express anything a metric author wants, independent of what objectives ship, but duplicates every template `pricing-core` already implements and certifies, and a `capped_gamma` metric and a `capped_gamma` objective would drift the moment one catalogue is edited without the other. **An `expression`-only path for metrics:** the same AST grammar (§4.6) reused for values instead of gradients, but Phase 1 has not shipped `expression` objectives (OQ-573) and a metric-only exception to that gate is a second thing to keep off. **Name an `ObjectiveTemplate` and its parameters, reusing the template's loss as the metric's value (exposure-weighted mean):** no second catalogue, no second grammar, and the loss is the one FR-143's analytic gradient/hessian were already certified against — a metric inherits that certification history rather than starting its own. |  |  |  |
| OQ-591 | open question |  | ~~Does a Custom Metric define its own value computation — a metric-specific template catalogue or `expression` grammar — or does it name an existing `ObjectiveTemplate` (§4.5) and reuse that template's loss?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-19: a metric names an `ObjectiveTemplate` and reuses its loss, evaluated as an exposure-weighted mean — FR-155**, on OQ-573's Phase-1-templates-only rule. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-592 | open question |  | ~~§5.1 declared one `POST /custom-metrics` row and left it "not built, deferred to Phase 1b" (FR-154). Now that a metric gates early stopping (FR-160), should this slice ship create only, or the full six-endpoint set FR-166 built for `custom-objectives` — read, certify, read-certificate, submit, usage?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-19: all six.** | Raised 2026-08-19 in the custom-metrics decision gate. FR-166 was itself a correction: §5.1 originally declared five `custom-objectives` write endpoints and no way to read what they wrote, and an Approver who could not fetch a certificate was being asked to approve a verdict they could not see. **Create only, deferring the rest:** repeats exactly the gap FR-166 closed, this time known in advance rather than found by a later audit — certification is a 202 job, so a caller with only `POST /custom-metrics` has no way to learn whether a metric it created ever certified. **All six, mirroring `custom-objectives`' shape (`FR-142/146/163/164/166`) exactly:** a metric now sits on the fitting path's critical edge — it decides when boosting stops — so it needs the same governance visibility an objective has, not a lesser version of it because it shipped later. |  |  |  |
| OQ-592 | open question |  | ~~§5.1 declared one `POST /custom-metrics` row, not built and deferred to Phase 1b (FR-154). Now that a metric gates early stopping (FR-160), should this slice ship create only, or the full six-endpoint set FR-166 built for `custom-objectives`?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-19: all six — FR-162**, the same argument FR-166 made for objectives: an approver who cannot fetch a certificate is being asked to approve a verdict they cannot see. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-593 | open question |  | ~~LightGBM evaluates its builtin metrics before `feval`. When a Model Spec declares a builtin in `eval_metrics` *and* early-stops on a Custom Metric, `_fit_lightgbm` sets `params["metric"] = "None"` and the declared builtin is silently dropped — accepted by the spec, never evaluated, and nothing on the returned `GbmFit` tells the caller; XGBoost reports both. Does a *documented* silent drop satisfy FR-159's requirement that `eval_metrics` be honoured?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-21: record the drop on the fit — FR-161.** | Found 2026-08-20 in the final review of the custom-metrics branch (FR-154), immediately before merge. Already tested (`test_lightgbm_drops_a_builtin_eval_metric_rather_than_stop_on_it`, `packages/pricing-core/tests/test_gbm.py`) and named in FR-160's 2026-08-20 amendment, so the asymmetry is documented rather than hidden — but FR-159's own parenthetical describes the defect it was raised against as "a spec accepted, silently ignored, and reported to the caller as configured," which is close to what still happens here in one narrow combination. **Refuse the combination**, with a named `GbmFitError` at spec-validation or fit time: closest to FR-159's intent — the caller learns immediately rather than receiving a curve missing a metric it asked for — but costs a refusal for a spec that would otherwise fit fine on XGBoost. **Record the drop on the fit result** — a field or note on `GbmFit` naming which declared metrics were not evaluated and why: the spec is still accepted, and the caller is told, at the cost of a new shape on the contract. **Leave it as-is**, on the grounds that it is tested and documented in FR-160: costs nothing, but leaves the caller uninformed at runtime unless it consults the spec. A fourth option — reimplementing LightGBM's builtin in Python as an `feval` entry so both are reported — was considered and rejected in review: it would emit a curve labelled e.g. `rmse` that is not LightGBM's own `rmse`, and FR-159 requires builtin names to be "passed to the backend's own metric vocabulary." |  |  |  |

| OQ-595 | open question |  | ~~**`FactorIntent.OFFSET` is live, selectable and consumed by nothing — a Factor an actuary declares as an offset is fitted with a free coefficient.** FR-88 declares the intent; `rateable()` tests `risk` only, and neither the GLM nor the GBM fit path reads intent at all. Smallest change and it stops the mis-fit today, but it leaves FR-88 declaring an intent no mechanism honours. **(c) Supersede `FactorIntent.OFFSET`** as FR-209 supersedes the type, leaving `OffsetSpec` the single way to declare an offset. **(d) Leave it** — rejected on its face: a silent mis-fit is the one outcome none of the others produce. |  |  |  |

| OQ-596 | open question |  | ~~**The GBM partial-dependence sweep caps nothing and covers every factor, where FR-174 says declared ones. What bounds it?** Partial dependence runs one full-population scoring pass per grid point — ten quantiles for a numeric factor, but **every distinct level** for a categorical. At the 10 000 levels NFR-477 itself names, a single column costs 10 000 passes, roughly 1.9 hours, which a per-factor budget records as one factor. Raised 2026-08-22 from OQ-572's measurement, which attributed 90 % of the GBM diagnostics cost to this sweep.~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-22: (a) in substance with its mechanism replaced — the grid is capped at the 20 most-exposed levels and truncates rather than pools (FR-175); (b) withdrawn as a no-op.** A live `IndexError` found in the same two functions is FR-176 and OQ-598. | **(a) Cap the categorical grid** at the top N levels by exposure, pooling the remainder and recording the pooling as evidence. Bounds the cost and keeps the shape readable; the pooled bar is a summary an actuary must be told about. **(b) Honour FR-174's "declared factors"** so the sweep covers what the caller asked for rather than everything. Cheapest, and it is what the spec already says. **(c) Bound the budget instead**: state that NFR-488 does not hold above a named level count. Honest, and it documents the cliff without removing it. **(d) Leave it uncapped** — the status quo, which makes a 60-factor diagnostics run unbounded in the level count of its worst column. |  |  |  |
| OQ-596 | open question |  | ~~**The GBM partial-dependence sweep caps nothing and covers every factor, where FR-174 says declared ones. What bounds it?** Partial dependence runs one full-population scoring pass per grid point — ten quantiles for a numeric factor, but **every distinct level** for a categorical. At the 10 000 levels NFR-477 itself names, one column costs 10 000 passes, roughly 1.9 hours, which a per-factor budget records as a single factor. Raised 2026-08-22 from OQ-572's measurement, which attributed 90 % of the GBM diagnostics cost to this sweep.~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-22: the grid is capped at the 20 most-exposed levels and the omission recorded (FR-175); the "covers every factor" half is withdrawn.** The sweep already covers exactly the declared factors — the worker passes `spec.factors` — so that half named no gap. The recommendation's pooled bar is **not implementable**: a pooled "other" is an unseen level and FR-131 refuses scoring one, so the cap truncates and names what it dropped.
