Needs a design-matrix path that both fit paths respect, which is what OQ-595 rejected for `offset` on cost. **(b) A second name for `control`** — fitted, never rateable. Costs nothing and makes the arm redundant, which is an argument for (c). **(c) Supersede it**, as FR-84 supersedes `offset`, leaving a two-arm enum that says exactly what it means. **(d) Leave it refused indefinitely** — rejected on its face: FR-85 is explicitly an interim, and a refusal with no owner is the silence §13 rule 1 forbids. |  |  |  |

The capability it named is real and is re-sited on the Model Spec, gated and owned by WK-690. FR-85's ground for keeping it open is corrected in place. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-598 | open question |  | ~~**What should permutation importance and partial dependence report for an `interaction` Factor?** A cross sources no column of its own, so neither block has a column to permute or to hold. FR-176 makes both skip it and record the skip, having first made a GBM able to fit a cross at all — the crash that hid this question was in the encoder, not the diagnostics. Raised 2026-08-22 while deciding OQ-596.~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-22: (a) in substance with its mechanism restated — the cross is permuted and swept *jointly through its operands* (FR-177) — and the second half resolved by skipping the operands (FR-178), which turns out to fix a live crash rather than save a scoring pass.** | **(a) Operate on the cross's resolved term** — permute the combined column, sweep its cells. Treats the interaction as the single feature the design matrix says it is, and is the smallest change. The cell count is the product of the operands' level counts, so FR-175's cap matters more here, not less. **(b) Operate on the operands jointly** — permute both source columns under one shuffle, sweep the grid of operand pairs. Closer to what an actuary reads off a two-way, and it re-introduces the operands whose main effects FR-92 excludes as collinear with the cross. **Either way the question has a second half**: an operand carries a source column, so it is already swept and reported as though it were a model factor — at full scoring cost, for a term the booster has no column for — while the GLM's type-III block skips exactly those operands and states why. The two paths disagree today and this question owns the disagreement. **(c) Report neither for a cross**, and say so in the artifact — the interim FR-176 ships, made permanent. Honest and leaves a reviewer with no per-factor evidence for a term that may carry a lot of the model. **(d) Leave the `IndexError`** — rejected on its face. |  |  |  |
| OQ-598 | open question |  | ~~**What should permutation importance and partial dependence report for an `interaction` Factor?** A cross sources no column of its own, so neither block has a column to permute or to hold; FR-176 makes both skip it and record the skip, having first made a GBM able to fit a cross at all. Permuting or sweeping the operands jointly is the obvious candidate and is not obviously the right one — the operands' own main effects are collinear with the cross by FR-92's argument. **The operands raise a second half of the same question**: they carry source columns, so each is swept and reported as though it were a model factor, at full scoring cost, for a term the booster has no column for — while the GLM's type-III block skips exactly those operands and says why. The two paths disagree, and this question owns the disagreement. Raised 2026-08-22 while deciding OQ-596.~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-22: the cross is measured jointly through its operands — FR-177 — and the operands themselves are skipped — FR-178.** A joint shuffle *is* a permutation of the resolved cross column, so the two candidate mechanisms are one on that half; they differ only on the sweep grid, where the observed cells are scoreable and the Cartesian product is refused by FR-131. The second half was understated: an operand swept alone is not a wasted pass but a **live crash** on any sparse cross. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-599 | open question |  | ~~**`fit_glm`'s `seed` keyword is read by nothing, and `02` §5.2 publishes it.** NFR-481 asks for reproducibility under "identical `spec_hash` **+ seed**", and the parameter a caller would reach for to supply that seed is inert: `grep '\bseed\b' glm.py` returns the declaration and two `spec.seed` uses in the CV path, there is no `**kwargs`, and `GeneralizedLinearRegressor` is constructed with no `random_state`. `spec.seed` is the live one. **Six non-test callers pass the argument** — `model_handlers.py:371` (the real fit path), `diagnostics.py:504`, `transparency.py:199` and three in `scripts/bench-model.py` — and five test sites pass `seed=1` against a spec seed of `0`, a disagreement silently discarded. It is unobservable today only because every one of those five leaves `select_by` at `"fixed"`, so `_fit_cv_path` never runs and no RNG is consulted. **The repository already documents the fact, in the wrong file**: `fit_ebm` carries the identical dead parameter and its docstring (`ebm.py:111-114`) calls it "`fit_glm`'s vestigial kwarg for call-site symmetry … deliberately **not** the reproducibility source", while `glm.py` and `02` §5.2 say nothing. Raised 2026-08-22 by the closure slice while evidencing NFR-481's GLM half.~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-22: option (b) — the parameter is removed from `fit_glm` and `fit_ebm` and from `02` §5.2; `spec.seed` is the single seed for every fitter. Specified as FR-179.** | **(a) Document it as vestigial**, in `glm.py`'s docstring and beside the §5.2 signature, matching what `fit_ebm` already says. Cheapest, truthful, and changes no call site — but it leaves a parameter whose only purpose is to be ignored, and the next reader still has to be told. **(b) Remove it** from `fit_glm` and `fit_ebm`, and from §5.2. Removes the trap at the source and makes `spec.seed` the single seed, matching `fit_gbm`, which never had the parameter and reads `spec.seed` directly. It is a **breaking change to a published signature** across nineteen call sites, six of them outside tests. **(c) Honour it** — let an explicit `seed=` override `spec.seed` for the fit. Makes the published contract true, and is the only option that gives NFR-481's "+ seed" clause an instrument. But it creates a second seed that is *not* in `spec_hash`, so two fits with identical digests could differ — which is the precise thing NFR-481 exists to forbid, and `GlmCvSpec`'s docstring already refuses on that ground. **(d) Leave it** — the status quo, in which the contract advertises a knob that does nothing. |  |  |  |
| OQ-599 | open question |  | ~~**`fit_glm`'s `seed` keyword is read by nothing, and §5.2 publishes it.** NFR-481 asks for reproducibility under "identical `spec_hash` + seed", and the parameter a caller would supply that seed through is inert; `spec.seed` is the live one. Six non-test callers pass it, five test sites pass a value that disagrees with their spec's, and `fit_ebm`'s docstring already records the fact for its own copy of the same dead parameter. Raised 2026-08-22 while evidencing NFR-481's GLM half.~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-22: option (b) — removed from `fit_glm` and `fit_ebm` and from §5.2; `spec.seed` is the single seed. FR-179.** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-600 | open question |  | ~~`objective-certificate.result.checks` publishes `minItems: 8`; `CertificateResult` requires `1`. Neither number is right, and the shared type is why.~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-23: option (a), specified as `02` FR-158.** Found 2026-08-22 by W32-1's scalar-constraint guard, on its first run. | `02` §4.7's dated 2026-08-18 amendment says **"All nine checks are emitted for every template, always"**, and the authored contract's own `$comment` from that same amendment calls `branch_discontinuity` a **ninth** named check (FR-148) and adds it to the `name` enum — while leaving `minItems: 8` untouched. So **8 is a stale pre-amendment count, contradicted by the file it sits in** (nine enum members, a floor of eight). But the model's `1` cannot simply become `9`: `CertificateResult` is **shared** — `objectives.py:691` for `ObjectiveCertificate` and `metrics.py:213` for `MetricCertificate` — and FR-157 gives a metric certificate **four** checks, reusing §4.7's vocabulary unchanged. `min_length=9` on the shared type would refuse every metric certificate the spec requires. **(a)** Per-artifact obligation: leave `CertificateResult` unbounded, enforce nine on `ObjectiveCertificate` and four on `MetricCertificate` where each is constructed, and correct the contract to `minItems: 9`. **(b)** Split the shared type into two. **(c)** Leave the model loose and correct only the contract to `9`, accepting that the floor is published and unenforced. |  |  |  |
| OQ-600 | open question |  | ~~`objective-certificate.result.checks` publishes `minItems: 8`; `CertificateResult` requires `1`.~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-23: option (a) — the shared container stays unbounded and each artifact enforces its own count, specified as `FR-158`; the contract is corrected to `minItems: 9`.** Original text: **`objective-certificate.result.checks` publishes `minItems: 8`; `CertificateResult` requires `1`. Neither number was right, and the shared type is why.** Found 2026-08-22 by W32-1's scalar-constraint guard on its first run. §4.7's dated 2026-08-18 amendment says **"All nine checks are emitted for every template, always"**, and the authored contract's own `$comment` from that amendment calls `branch_discontinuity` a **ninth** named check (FR-148) and adds it to the `name` enum — while leaving `minItems: 8` untouched, so **8 is a stale count contradicted by the file it sits in**. The model's `1` cannot simply become `9` either: `CertificateResult` is **shared** between `ObjectiveCertificate` and `MetricCertificate`, and FR-157 gives a metric certificate **four** checks — `min_length=9` would refuse every metric certificate the spec requires. Options and a recommendation ((a) a per-artifact obligation, leaving the shared container unbounded) are in [`../open-questions.md`](open-questions.md). Scoped out of the guard meanwhile by `UNRESOLVED_CONSTRAINT_DISAGREEMENTS`, whose companion test fails the moment the disagreement is settled. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-601 | open question |  | ~~**What evidence stands beside an interaction candidate?**~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-23: option (b) — a holdout strength ratio, specified as `02` FR-168.** FR-135 and OQ-578 both promise "exposure share and holdout lift — the two numbers that say whether a suggestion is worth taking and over how much of the book". Neither is real. `ShapInteraction.exposure_share` is written as the literal `1.0` and defaults to `1.0` on the type, and it cannot be anything else: every row has a value for both features, so a pair's observed cells cross to the whole book. Holdout lift appears in no section of any spec, on no shape and in no computation. Raised 2026-08-23 by the WK-664 slice-map backlog (item 3). | The disanalogy with the shares FR-181 corrected is structural: a partial-dependence point and a worst region are *per level*, so their shares partition exposure and a share below one means something; a candidate pair is a single object spanning the entire frame. So the **"over how much of the book" half of the promise is not answerable per pair at all** and is withdrawn rather than redefined. What remains open is *worth taking*, against the `strength` the ranker already computes — the mean absolute sum of the pair's off-diagonal TreeSHAP entries, on a capped in-sample slice. **(a) Strength alone.** Truthful and free; an actuary sees the ranking and nothing about whether it survives out of sample. **(b) A holdout strength ratio** — recompute the same statistic on the holdout split and publish it against the in-sample value, so a pair that is a fitting artefact shows as a collapse. **(c) Concentration** — the share of *exposure* carried by rows in the top decile of absolute interaction contribution, which is the nearest honest thing to "how much of the book". **(d) A/E spread across the cross's cells on holdout**, above an exposure floor. |  |  |  |
| OQ-601 | open question |  | ~~**What evidence stands beside an interaction candidate, now that its exposure share is vacuous and its holdout lift is defined nowhere?**~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-23: option (b) — a holdout strength ratio, specified as FR-168.** FR-135 promised an actuary two numbers saying "whether a suggestion is worth taking and over how much of the book", and neither exists: the share is `1.0` for every pair by construction, and holdout lift has no definition, no field and no computation. Raised 2026-08-23 by the WK-664 slice-map backlog (item 3), which found the constant. The "over how much of the book" half of the question is not answerable per pair at all — a pair spans the whole frame — so what is open is the *worth taking* half. Options and the reasoning are in [`../open-questions.md`](open-questions.md). The surviving half of the question — *worth taking* — is answered by recomputing the statistic the ranker already computes on the holdout the pipeline already loads; the *over how much of the book* half was withdrawn as unanswerable per pair rather than redefined. Until the suggestion panel is built the artifact publishes strength alone. *(Amended 2026-08-25: the backend half is built (W6b-5a) and the artifact carries `holdout_strength_ratio`; the panel is `W6b-5b`'s. `OQ-608` records that the ratio's null is not `1`.)* |  |  |  |  |

Raised 2026-08-24 by W32-8, which had no standing to resolve it: spec-versus-spec is what `CLAUDE.md` §0 reserves. | **(a) Add the two missing §5.3 rows** — a Custom metric library and a Peril structure library — making the opening sentence true and giving `WK-664` two more screens, both already backed by §5.1's own endpoint rows. **(b) Amend FR-167's wording** to "the three libraries §5.1 exposes, of which §5.3 renders one", making the view layer's asymmetry deliberate. |  |  |  |

W32-8 built the endpoint table's reading — the field on two libraries, absent on the third — and asserted the absence rather than letting it be implicit. | OQ-603 | open question |  | ~~Which artifact rows carry `usage_count`?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-24: option (a), by the WK-692 closure proposal's Part D item 4 — the prose is qualified to two libraries.** FR-167's prose was unqualified while §5.1 omits the field from peril structures; W32-8 built §5.1's reading and asserted the absence. The ground: a Model Spec cannot reference a Peril Structure — the reference runs the other way (`perils.py:214-228`) — so "the count of Model Specs referencing that artifact" is `0` by construction on a peril row. | OQ-604 | open question |  | ~~**Does FR-141's `-approx` slug rule bind every surrogate, or only the ones the transparency Job builds?** Raised 2026-08-24 by W6b-1a's residue review. FR-141 states the rule unconditionally — *"the surrogate's `model_family_slug` is the source model's own family slug with `-approx` appended"* — while its own enforcement half is scoped to one caller: an over-long slug is *"refused by name … before the transparency Job spends any compute fitting it"*. The requirement is inconsistent with itself before any code is read, and the platform holds the narrow reading. `approximation_spec` (`packages/pricing-core/src/pricing_core/modelling/transparency.py:94`) is the only writer of the suffix; its one caller is `backend/src/app/worker/model_handlers.py:970`, and the length refusal at `:976` sits on that same path. Nothing binds a **client-declared** surrogate: `reserve_model` (`backend/src/app/platform/modelling.py:340`) persists `model_family_slug=spec.model_family_slug` verbatim at `:426`, and `_refuse_mismatched_approximation` (called at `:377`) compares `dataset_version_id`, `split_ref` and `factors` and not the slug. That omission is **absent, not hidden**: the sibling `interval_for` guard ~90 lines earlier builds the same mismatch list with **four** fields, `model_family_slug` first (`:583`), against the approximation guard's three (`:669-673`). Across `backend/src packages frontend/src` the literal `-approx` has five occurrences and not one of them enforces or reads it: the constructor, one error message (`model_handlers.py:976`), one test slug, one fixture comment, and one assertion under `packages/model-schema/tests/`. *(Use the plain `packages` prefix. `packages/*/src` is not a prefix match and silently drops the constructor, leaving a clean three that reads as the suffix never being built in `pricing-core` at all — a false negative pointing the way that dismisses this row.)* FR-141's headline survives — the iff at the type makes a surrogate identifiable *as* a surrogate — but not *whose* surrogate it is, which is what the frontend already renders as a bare unresolved id.~~ | **(a) Enforce the suffix on the create path**: validate a client-supplied `model_family_slug` against the source model's in `reserve_model` and refuse a mismatch. **(b) Scope FR-141's slug sentence to the transparency Job**, matching the wording to the platform and leaving a client-declared surrogate free to be named anything. Note this is a spec change that code cannot then honour selectively: worker and API enter through the same `reserve_model`, whose audit hardcodes `source=JobSource.API` (`modelling.py:442`) where `:831` in the same file distinguishes via `JobSource.SYSTEM if job_id else JobSource.API` — the seam a worker-only rule needs exists as a pattern but not on this path. **(c) Derive the slug server-side** for any spec carrying `approximates_model_id`, so the property holds on every route by construction rather than by validation. **Cost of (c), measured not assumed:** no extra query. `_refuse_mismatched_approximation` at `:377` already fetches the source spec and the slug is written at `:426`, same function and same transaction (`reserve_model` at `:340` is the only `def` between `:330` and `:440`). One real cost remains: the 64-character refusal must move *with* the derivation, or an over-long derived slug hits the `String(64)` column at the DB layer instead of being refused by name as the FR promises. **Option (c) resolves the slug half only.** The version half is untouched by all three — `approximates_model_id` has no companion version field, `interval_for` carries `model_version`, and `/models/:slug` resolves to latest — so a link derived from a slug alone can land on a source version the surrogate does not approximate. That needs a companion field whichever option is taken, and so is not a reason to prefer one. |  |  |  |
| OQ-604 | open question |  | ~~**Does FR-141's `-approx` slug rule bind every surrogate, or only the ones the transparency Job builds?** The requirement asserts it unconditionally while its own enforcement clause is scoped to the transparency Job, and the platform holds the narrow reading: `reserve_model` persists a caller-supplied `model_family_slug` verbatim and its approximation guard compares three fields, where the sibling `interval_for` guard compares four and leads with the slug.~~ Options: enforce on the create path, scope the sentence to the Job, or derive the slug server-side. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (c) — the slug is derived server-side at reservation, the length refusal moves with it, the worker's refusal becomes unreachable (FR-141 amended); the version half is OQ-613.** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-605 | open question |  | ~~Should `GET /api/v1/custom-objectives` filter by **applicability** server-side? FR-153 makes an objective applicable to particular responses and backends, and a Model Spec pairing them wrongly is refused at spec validation — so an objective picker that offers inapplicable objectives manufactures the error the requirement exists to prevent. `CustomObjective` carries `applicability` on the row (`backends`, `responses`, `offset_required`, `y_domain` — `model_schema/objectives.py`), so a client *can* filter. But the route's query parameters are `status`, `slug`, `cursor` and `limit` only, and the list is cursor-paginated with `DEFAULT_LIMIT` 50 and `MAX_LIMIT` 200. A picker filtering the page it happens to hold therefore renders "no applicable objectives" while applicable ones sit on a later page, and it does so **silently**: an empty picker is indistinguishable from a workspace that owns none. It is invisible in every test fixture and in the demo workspace, and it appears first in the workspace holding the most artifacts — the one least able to check the answer by eye. Raised 2026-08-25 by `W6b-4a`'s derivation; the finding is allocated to `W6b-4b`, which builds the custom arm, and the question is filed a cycle early so the answer exists before that slice needs it. Mirrored in `docs/specs/02-modelling.md` §10.~~ | **(a) The client fetches every page before filtering, with a stated bound.** Correct at any size a picker can realistically load, and the bound is the point: an implementation that quietly stops paging has reproduced the exact defect this question exists to fix, a picker that cannot distinguish "none applicable" from "none seen". So the option is only well-formed with the cap written into it — **fetch at most N pages at `limit=200`, and when `next_cursor` is still non-null at the cap, say so in the picker** rather than presenting a filtered list as complete. A visible truncation is a different artifact from a silent one. Costs up to N requests when the picker opens. **(b) One page at `limit=200`, with the same disclosure.** One request; the disclosure carries the entire correctness burden, and a caveat under a list is read less often than the list. **(c) An `applicable_to` parameter on the route** — the response kind and backend, composing with the `status` filter already there. The server holds the whole set, so the answer is exact and one request, and the pagination question disappears rather than being managed. It is a `02` §5.1 change plus backend work. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (a) as the interim, (c) as the durable answer** |  |  |  |
| OQ-605 | open question |  | ~~Should `GET /api/v1/custom-objectives` filter by~~ **applicability** server-side? FR-153 makes an objective applicable to particular responses and backends, and a spec pairing them wrongly is refused at validation, so a picker offering inapplicable objectives manufactures the error the requirement prevents. `CustomObjective` carries `applicability` on the row, so a client can filter — but the query is `status`, `slug`, `cursor`, `limit` only, and the list is cursor-paginated (`DEFAULT_LIMIT` 50, `MAX_LIMIT` 200). A picker filtering the page it holds renders "no applicable objectives" while applicable ones sit on a later page, **silently**: an empty picker is indistinguishable from a workspace owning none, which makes it invisible in every fixture and worst in the largest workspace. Options are a bounded client-side fetch-all that **states when it stopped paging with `next_cursor` still non-null** (a visible truncation being a different artifact from a silent one), a single large page with the same disclosure, or an `applicable_to` parameter on the route. Recommendation is split: the bounded client fetch as the interim, owned by `W6b-4b`; the route parameter as the durable answer. **That durable answer has no owner** — it is a `02` §5.1 change plus backend work, `WK-664` is a frontend workstream, and `WK-692`, which owned Phase 1b's backend half and built this route under FR-167, is closed. It therefore needs a new workstream or a later phase, which is a maintainer's decision about the plan. Raised 2026-08-25 by `W6b-4a`'s derivation, filed a cycle before `W6b-4b` needs it. **Open**, owned by the maintainer; options and evidence are in `docs/open-questions.md`. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (a) as the interim, (c) as the durable answer** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-606 | open question |  | ~~**Does `POST /api/v1/model-specs/validate` answer `422` for a spec that merely cannot be fitted?** §5.1's row for the route states the rule without qualification — *"A spec that merely cannot be fitted is not a bad **request**, so it is not a 4xx; a spec naming a version that does not exist is a 404"* — and the handler's own docstring gives the reason: a 422 *"would say the request was malformed, and `02` §5.3's live validation would then be a form that errors on every keystroke while the actuary is still typing."* But `ModelSpecValidate.spec` is typed as the `ModelSpec` union, so FastAPI parses it **before the handler runs** and `GlmSpec`'s three `model_validator(mode="after")` guards raise at parse time: `_frequency_declares_its_exposure` (`family == "poisson"` with `offset.kind == "none"`), `_a_tweedie_power_lies_between_the_two_families_it_spans` (a power outside 1 to 2 exclusive), and `_a_surrogate_says_so_in_both_places`. The route declares `responses=problems(401, 403, 404, 422)` and returns 422 for all three. **A Poisson spec with no offset merely cannot be fitted, and the two statements cannot both be true.** WK-661's closure record (`docs/roadmap.md`:2487) claims the 200-not-4xx behaviour as *delivered*, so the contradiction sits in a closure record and not only in a spec row. **Measured 2026-08-25, authenticated and DB-backed** (unauthenticated returns 401 — auth resolves before body validation, so an unauthenticated probe confirms nothing here): the 422 is `application/problem+json`, `code` `VALIDATION_FAILED`, `title` *"Request validation failed"*, `detail` *"1 field(s) failed validation."*, and the actionable sentence — which names FR-111 — appears **only** in `errors[].message`, under `field: "spec.glm"`, a union path rather than a form field. The 404 arm returns `errors: []` with its meaning in `title`/`detail`. It is **not** a duplicate of `SpecProblemKind.OFFSET_MISSING`, whose only emitter fires when a *declared* offset column is absent from the dataset version — a different condition, live and correctly reachable; every other `MODEL_OFFSET_MISSING` in the tree is an unrelated scoring-time error code. Raised 2026-08-25 by `W6b-4a`, which is building the very §5.3 form the docstring's reasoning is about. Mirrored in `docs/specs/02-modelling.md` §10.~~ | **(a) The route accepts the body unparsed and re-validates it through the same `ModelSpec` union**, catching `ValidationError` and turning each error into a `SpecProblem`, so a structurally-invalid spec returns 200 with the complete list. Honours §5.1 as written and gives the form one channel. **This does not require hand-writing the union** — re-validating through the declared `ModelSpec` writes no shape twice, so `CLAUDE.md` §2's divergence rule is not engaged; what it costs is the typed request body on this one route and the generated OpenAPI request schema that comes with it. **(b) Amend the §5.1 row** so that a spec violating a contract-level invariant is a 422 and only the service's own checks return 200 with problems. No code changes, and 422 becomes a first-class validation channel. The cost is that the boundary becomes "wherever a validator happens to live", which is not a rule a client can predict or a test can pin, and the frontend must render two error shapes permanently. **(c) Move the family-keyed validators out of `GlmSpec` into `validate_spec`**, emitting `SpecProblem`s, and add the matching refusal on `POST /models`. **This is the established pattern on this exact route pair:** `complexity_or_refuse` (`backend/src/app/platform/model_specs.py`:90) is FR-185's single check, documented as *"One implementation, two presentations — the alternative is two thresholds that drift"*, reported as a problem by `validate_spec` and turned into a refusal by `enforce_complexity` (`:470`). So (c) does **not** withdraw the storability guard as first thought; it relocates it from the type to the create path, where FR-185's already sits. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (a) — unparsed body, re-validated through ModelSpec, 200 with the complete SpecProblem list** |  |  |  |
| OQ-606 | open question |  | ~~**Does `POST /api/v1/model-specs/validate` answer `422` for a spec that merely cannot be fitted?**~~ §5.1's row states the rule without qualification — *"A spec that merely cannot be fitted is not a bad **request**, so it is not a 4xx"* — and the handler's docstring gives the reason: a 422 *"would say the request was malformed, and §5.3's live validation would then be a form that errors on every keystroke."* But the request body is typed as the `ModelSpec` union, so FastAPI parses it before the handler runs and `GlmSpec`'s three `model_validator(mode="after")` guards raise at parse time — a Poisson spec with `offset.kind == "none"`, a Tweedie power outside 1 to 2 exclusive, and a surrogate whose response column disagrees with itself. The route declares 422 and returns it for all three, so a spec that merely cannot be fitted gets one. `docs/roadmap.md`:2487 claims the 200-not-4xx behaviour as WK-661-delivered, so the contradiction sits in a closure record too. Measured 2026-08-25: the actionable sentence, which names FR-111, is carried **only** in `errors[].message` — `title` and `detail` are generic. Not a duplicate of `SpecProblemKind.OFFSET_MISSING`, which fires on a *declared* offset column absent from the version. Options are an unparsed body re-validated through the same union so errors become `SpecProblem`s, amending this §5.1 row so a contract-level invariant is a 422, or moving the family-keyed validators into `validate_spec` with a matching refusal on `POST /models` — the last being FR-185's existing two-presentation shape rather than a loss of the storability guard, and costing instead the type-level unrepresentability that `CLAUDE.md` §7 argues an actuarial invariant deserves. Recommendation is the first, held with a narrow margin. **No owner:** all three are a §5.1 or backend change, `WK-664` is a frontend workstream, and `WK-692` — which owned Phase 1b's backend half and built this route — is closed, so it needs a new workstream or a later phase. **Nothing is blocked meanwhile** — `W6b-4a` renders and tests the 200, 404 and 422 branches distinctly. Raised 2026-08-25 by `W6b-4a`. **Open**, owned by the maintainer; options and evidence are in `docs/open-questions.md`. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (a) — unparsed body, re-validated through ModelSpec, 200 with the complete SpecProblem list** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-607 | open question |  | ~~**`GbmSpec.objective` takes a closed set of four and `GbmSpec.eval_metrics` takes an open one, through the same type — so nothing in the contract says which objectives the platform supports.** FR-120's set is `_OBJECTIVES` / `SUPPORTED_GBM_OBJECTIVES` at `packages/pricing-core/src/pricing_core/modelling/gbm.py`:107-115, whose own comment states the hazard: *"Two hand-written lists would eventually disagree about which objectives the platform supports, and the disagreement would show up as a spec that validated and then failed."* But `model_schema/modelling.py` types both positions as `GbmFunctionRef` — :1321 `objective`, :1345 `eval_metrics` — and `GbmFunctionRef.name` is deliberately open (:1210-1212): *"Not an enum — the eval-metric vocabulary is the backend's and differs between them, and a closed set here would refuse a metric XGBoost supports."* The reason is sound for metrics and does not apply to the objective arm, but one type serves both. `model-schema` depends on `pydantic` alone, so it cannot import the set. The contract therefore carries it nowhere machine-readable: `docs/contracts/openapi/generated.json` has zero occurrences of `count:poisson`, and the hand-authored `docs/contracts/schemas/model-spec.schema.json`:192 lists the four names inside a *description string* ending in an ellipsis, explicitly *"Not an enum"*. `W6b-4a` consequently authored `BUILTIN_GBM_OBJECTIVES` in `ModelSpecBuilderView.vue` — the second hand-written list the `pricing-core` comment warns against, and the divergence it predicts is silent. **Widened 2026-08-25 (W6b-4b): the same defect has a second surface.** `FITTABLE_OBJECTIVE_STATUSES` (`model_schema/objectives.py`:174) is `{certified, review, approved}` — the statuses a fit accepts, enforced at `gbm.py`:532, and deliberately *not* R4's `approved`-alone rule for a model reaching approval. `ObjectiveStatus` is a generated schema so the **type** reaches every client, but the **subset** reaches none: a picker that offers fittable objectives must either import a set the contract does not carry or hand-write a fourth copy. **Option (a) does not transfer to this surface** — a subset of an enum's members is not a field type, so narrowing a position cannot express it; only (b), an endpoint, or (c), a divergence test, apply. `W6b-4b` extends its divergence test to read `objectives.py` as well as `gbm.py`. Mirrored in `docs/specs/02-modelling.md` §10.~~ | **(a) A closed type for the objective position only** — `GbmSpec.objective` gets its own ref type whose `name` is a `Literal` over FR-120's four, while `eval_metrics` keeps `GbmFunctionRef` open. The set then reaches the generated contract and every client derives it. `GbmFunctionRef`'s docstring objects in advance — *"Two classes with identical fields would be a shape defined twice"* — but under (a) the fields are **not** identical: `Literal[…] \| None` against `str \| None` is exactly the distinction the two positions need, so §2's rule is not the one being broken. A `model-schema` change. **(b) An endpoint publishing the set** — a `02` §5.1 addition, and it makes a compile-time fact a runtime fetch. **(c) Keep the frontend list and pin it with a divergence test** — buildable now, but neither CI workflow triggers on the other's half (`python.yml` has no `frontend/**`, `frontend.yml` has no `packages/**`), so it needs a frontend-side test reading `gbm.py` as text **and** a `paths` addition to `frontend.yml`, on the precedent already set there for `docs/contracts/openapi/**`. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (a) — GbmSpec.objective gets a Literal-typed ref; eval_metrics stays open** |  |  |  |
| OQ-607 | open question |  | ~~**`GbmSpec.objective` takes a closed set of four and `GbmSpec.eval_metrics` takes an open one, through the same `GbmFunctionRef` — so nothing in the contract says which objectives the platform supports.**~~ FR-120's set lives in `pricing-core` (`gbm.py`:107-115), whose comment states the hazard: *"Two hand-written lists would eventually disagree about which objectives the platform supports, and the disagreement would show up as a spec that validated and then failed."* `GbmFunctionRef.name` is open for a metric reason that does not apply to the objective arm, and `model-schema` depends on `pydantic` alone so it cannot import the set. The contract carries it nowhere machine-readable — zero occurrences in `generated.json`, and the hand-authored `model-spec.schema.json` lists the four names in a description string ending in an ellipsis, explicitly *"Not an enum"*. `W6b-4a` therefore authored `BUILTIN_GBM_OBJECTIVES` in the frontend, the second hand-written list that comment warns against. Options are a closed type for the objective position only, an endpoint publishing the set, or keeping the frontend list behind a divergence test; the first is recommended, since it removes the second list rather than policing it. **No owner** — the first two are `model-schema` or `02` §5.1 changes and `WK-692` is closed. **Nothing is blocked meanwhile**: `W6b-4b` ships the divergence test. **Widened 2026-08-25: a second surface.** `FITTABLE_OBJECTIVE_STATUSES` (`model_schema/objectives.py`:174) is the `{certified, review, approved}` set a fit accepts, distinct from R4's `approved`-alone rule for a model reaching approval. `ObjectiveStatus` is generated so the type reaches clients; the subset does not, so a picker offering fittable objectives hand-writes a fourth copy. **Option (a) does not transfer** — a subset of an enum's members is not a field type — so only the endpoint or the divergence test apply here. Raised 2026-08-25 by `W6b-4b`. *(Amended 2026-08-25, W6b-5b: **(c)'s CI prerequisite is shipped.** That option needed a frontend-side test reading `gbm.py` as text **and** a `paths` addition so it runs on a `pricing-core` edit. `frontend.yml` now carries `packages/pricing-core/src/pricing_core/modelling/**` on **both** triggers, added by `W6b-4b`, and `W6b-5b` relies on the same path for a second such guard (`factorIntents.test.ts`, `OQ-610`). So (c) no longer costs a CI change for any future guard over that directory — which lowers its cost relative to (a) and (b) without changing the recommendation, since (c) still leaves the frontend authoring a modelling fact.)* **Open**, owned by the maintainer; options and evidence are in `docs/open-questions.md`. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (a) — GbmSpec.objective gets a Literal-typed ref; eval_metrics stays open** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-608 | open question |  | ~~**FR-168's holdout strength ratio does not have `1` as its null, so "a ratio near `1` says the structure survives" reads a collapse into pairs that did not collapse.** Two mechanisms, and the second is the one that matters. **Unequal N:** `_pair_strengths` caps with `min(x.shape[0], _INTERACTION_ROW_CAP)` **per call** (`pricing_core/modelling/transparency.py`; the constant is `5_000`), so a 50 000-row training partition contributes 5 000 rows and a 3 000-row holdout contributes 3 000. FR-168 names "the same seed, the same row cap and the same encoding" and never equal N — and with a cap rather than a count, equal caps give unequal N whenever either partition is smaller than the cap. This widens the ratio's spread and does **not** shift its centre: `mean(\|·\|)` is a sample mean and is unbiased at any N. **Selection, which does shift it:** the published five are the top five *by in-sample strength* (`sort(...)` then `[:5]`), and the holdout is a lookup on those same five. So the denominator is a **selected maximum** — the largest of many noisy estimates, biased upward by the selection itself — while the numerator is an independent re-measurement of the same pair, and unbiased. Regression to the mean follows: **the expected ratio is below `1` even where the structure is identical in both partitions, and even at equal N.** The requirement's interpretive sentence — "a ratio near `1` says the structure survives out of sample; a collapse toward `0` says the pair is a fitting artefact" — is written as though `1` were the unbiased null, and under selection it is not. Raised 2026-08-25 by `W6b-5a`, which **built the requirement as written** and remedied nothing: the grammar makes comparability a conclusion drawn from three enumerated sames, so showing that inference invalid is a defect in the requirement rather than in a build that follows it. Mirrored in `docs/specs/02-modelling.md` §10.~~ | **(a) Equalise N** — cap both passes at the smaller partition's row count. **Partial, not a cure**: it narrows the spread and leaves the centre where selection put it. It is also not absorbable without a spec change, because lowering the training cap changes `strength` itself, and `strength` is what selects the top five — so it would silently re-rank candidates in every future artifact, and `strength` is a published field. **(b) Debias the denominator** — estimate the selection effect and correct for it, or compute the in-sample strength for the selected pairs on a held-back split of the training partition, so the denominator is not the value that did the selecting. Addresses the cause. Costs a third pass and a statistic the requirement does not currently define. **(c) Publish a null band rather than correcting** — state, alongside the ratio, what value to expect under no collapse, so a reader compares against the right baseline instead of against `1`. Cheapest and most honest about what is known; it changes the interpretive sentence rather than the number, and needs the band itself to be estimated. **(d) Rank the candidates on the holdout instead** — select and measure on different partitions. Removes the selection bias entirely but changes what FR-135 publishes, which is out of this question's scope. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (c), with (b) if a band cannot be estimated cheaply** |  |  |  |
| OQ-608 | open question |  | ~~**FR-168's holdout strength ratio does not have `1` as its null.**~~ The published five are the top five *by in-sample strength*, and the holdout is a lookup on those same five — so the denominator is a **selected maximum**, biased upward by the selection, while the numerator is an independent re-measurement and unbiased. The expected ratio therefore sits below `1` even where the structure is identical in both partitions, and **even at equal N**. A second, weaker mechanism compounds it: the row cap `_INTERACTION_ROW_CAP` is applied per call inside `_pair_strengths`, so a partition smaller than the cap contributes fewer rows and equal caps give unequal N — which widens the spread without shifting the centre, since `mean(\|·\|)` is unbiased at any N. The requirement's interpretive sentence — "a ratio near `1` says the structure survives out of sample" — is written as though `1` were the unbiased null, and it is not. Options are equalising N (partial, and not absorbable: lowering the training cap changes the published `strength` that does the selecting), debiasing the denominator, publishing a null band beside the ratio, or ranking on the holdout instead; the band is recommended, since the field supports a judgment and a judgment needs a baseline. Whichever is chosen, the interpretive sentence needs amending with it. **No owner in WK-664** — every remedy is a spec change and `WK-692` is closed. Raised 2026-08-25 by `W6b-5a`, which built the requirement as written. **Open**, owned by the maintainer; options and evidence are in `docs/open-questions.md`. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (c), with (b) if a band cannot be estimated cheaply** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-609 | open question |  | ~~**What does `deprecated` mean for a Custom Objective — may new specs select one, and do existing pinned uses continue?** `02` FR-163 names `deprecated` as the lifecycle's terminal state; FR-4 (nothing hard-deleted) backs the pinned-uses half — an existing pin keeps resolving. Nothing states whether a **new** spec — a new Model, Peril Structure or Rating Version — may select a `deprecated` version. `WF-702` D6 asserts *"existing pinned uses continue, new specs cannot select it"* with only FR-163 cited, which states neither half's second clause.~~ | **(a) Selection is forbidden.** `deprecated` is read/execute-only: existing pins continue, new specs cannot select. Requires a selection-time validation and an error code. **(b) Selection with a warning.** Deprecation is advisory for new work; no new enforcement, and the certification/approval gates already control quality. **(c) No selection semantics.** `deprecated` is a label only. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (a) — deprecated is read/execute-only: existing pins continue, new specs cannot select** |  |  |  |
| OQ-609 | open question |  | ~~**What does `deprecated` mean for a Custom Objective — may new specs select one, and do existing pinned uses continue?**~~ `02` FR-163 names `deprecated` as the lifecycle's terminal state; FR-4 (nothing hard-deleted) backs the pinned-uses half — an existing pin keeps resolving. Nothing states whether a **new** spec — a new Model, Peril Structure or Rating Version — may select a `deprecated` version. `WF-702` D6 asserts *"existing pinned uses continue, new specs cannot select it"* with only FR-163 cited, which states neither half's second clause. *Recommendation: (a) selection is forbidden — `deprecated` is read/execute-only, pins continue, new specs cannot select; costs a selection-time validation and an error code. (b) selection with a warning — advisory, no new enforcement. (c) no selection semantics — a label only. A label new work may ignore does no governance work, so (a).* Open; raised 2026-08-25 (WK-664 decision maker) by the WF-698…05 journey-prose sweep. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (a) — deprecated is read/execute-only: existing pins continue, new specs cannot select** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-610 | open question |  | ~~**`FactorIntent` publishes four arms, the platform honours two, and nothing machine-readable says which — while `POST /factors` accepts all four.** Two named surfaces, one remedy space, so one question. **First: the contract publishes no live/superseded distinction.** FR-84 supersedes `offset` as a Factor intent and FR-86 supersedes `diagnostic`, both on a layer argument — offsetness and diagnosis are properties of *one fit*, while `Factor.intent` belongs to a Factor defined against a Dataset and reused by every Model Spec naming it — and **both deliberately keep their arm in the published contract**, for artifacts already carrying them. So the union will never narrow to match, and a client waiting for the type to say which arms are live waits for ever. No permitted-subset constant exists anywhere in Python; the *complement* does, as `REFUSED_FACTOR_INTENTS` in `pricing-core/modelling/factors.py`, which is why `W6b-5b` hand-writes the refusal rather than the permission and pins it there by a text-reading test — a hand-written permitted pair would have no executable authority to check against, and a newly-live arm would vanish from a picker with nothing failing. **Second, and harder: creation does not refuse.** `REFUSED_FACTOR_INTENTS` is referenced **nowhere** under `backend/`; the only refusal is `resolve_factors`, on the fit/predict/diagnostics/transparency path. So `POST /factors` accepts a superseded intent, stores it, and audits it — and the Factor detonates later, at fit. \"Let the backend refuse\" is therefore not a fallback that exists, and a UI control is currently the only thing standing between an actuary and a stored artifact that cannot be used. Raised 2026-08-25 by `W6b-5b`, which ships the control. Mirrored in `docs/specs/02-modelling.md` §10.~~ | **(a) Refuse at creation** — `POST /factors` rejects a superseded intent with the reason `REFUSED_FACTOR_INTENTS` already carries per arm (the mapping holds a distinct sentence for each, deliberately, so a reader gets the one that applies). Smallest change, closes the harder half, and makes every client's control a convenience rather than a guard. Does **not** publish the distinction, so each client still hand-writes it. **(b) Publish the distinction in the contract** — a `live` flag, or a separate `LiveFactorIntent` union alongside the full one for reading existing artifacts. Every client then derives it, and no hand-written copy survives anywhere. Larger: a `model-schema` change, a contract regeneration, and a decision about what an artifact carrying a superseded arm deserializes as. **(c) Both** — (a) makes the platform refuse, (b) makes clients not offer. They are complementary rather than alternative: (a) alone leaves four clients hand-writing a pair, (b) alone leaves the API accepting what no client offers. **(d) Neither, and rely on the divergence test** — what ships today. Cheapest; leaves an accepted-then-detonating path open to anything that is not this frontend. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (c), (a) first if sequenced** |  |  |  |
| OQ-610 | open question |  | ~~**`FactorIntent` publishes four arms, the platform honours two, and `POST /factors` accepts all four.**~~ FR-84 supersedes `offset` and FR-86 `diagnostic`, both deliberately keeping the arm in the published contract, so the union will never narrow and no machine-readable permitted subset exists — only the complement, `REFUSED_FACTOR_INTENTS` in `pricing-core`. Harder: that constant is referenced **nowhere under `backend/`**, so the only refusal is `resolve_factors` on the fit path. A superseded intent is accepted, stored and audited, then detonates at fit — "let the backend refuse" is not a fallback that exists, and a UI control is currently the only guard. Two surfaces, one remedy space, so one question. Options are refusing at creation, publishing the live/superseded distinction, both, or neither; **both** is recommended, refusal first, since it closes a hole rather than a duplication. **No owner** — backend and `model-schema` surfaces, and `WK-692` is closed. `W6b-5b` ships the control and a divergence test. Raised 2026-08-25. **Open**, owned by the maintainer; options and evidence are in `docs/open-questions.md`. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (c), (a) first if sequenced** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-611 | open question |  | ~~**`GET /api/v1/models` cannot filter by dataset version, so a view scoped to one filters in the client over a paginated list.** The factor workbench is addressed by Dataset Version (`/factors/:datasetVersionId`), but interaction candidates live on a per-**Model** transparency artifact and a version may carry many models. The route's query is `family`, `status`, `cursor` and `limit`; `Model.dataset_version_id` is a top-level field, so the client *can* filter — but only over whatever pages it has walked. **A filter applied to one page renders "no models" while matches sit on a later page, and an empty selector is then indistinguishable from a dataset version that genuinely has none.** That conflation is the defect, not the extra requests. **This is `OQ-605`'s shape one route over** — there, `GET /custom-objectives` omits applicability, which FR-153 makes necessary for a picker — and the two are the only recorded instances: a sweep of `docs/` finds `OQ-565` and `OQ-552` to be missing *fields* rather than a missing filter *axis*, `02`:1747 a parameter-name mismatch since fixed, and `OQ-607` a missing machine-readable set. Filed per-instance rather than folded into a pattern row, because each names a different route and a different view, and a shared row would leave neither addressable. **A second, quieter consequence:** `Model` carries **no timestamp** — `created_at` is on `ModelRow` and does not reach the contract — so "the most recent model" is available *only* as the route's `ORDER BY id DESC` over UUIDv7 ids. A client that filters must therefore also preserve order, and nothing in the type system says so: a `sort`, a `Map` round-trip or an out-of-order page fetch degrades a "most recent" default to an arbitrary model with no error anywhere. Raised 2026-08-25 by `W6b-5b`. Mirrored in `docs/specs/02-modelling.md` §10.~~ | **(a) A `dataset_version_id` filter on `GET /models`** — the direct fix, composing with `family` and `status` as they already compose. A `02` §5.1 change plus backend work. It also removes the order-preservation hazard, because a filtered page is then the route's own answer rather than a client's reconstruction of one. **(b) A route on the version** — `GET /dataset-versions/{id}/models`, which reads more naturally from a version-scoped view and adds a second way to ask one question. **(c) Keep the client-side filter, bounded and disclosed** — walk to a stated cap and say so when a cursor survives it, so a truncated list is never presented as complete. Buildable now; it manages the defect rather than removing it, and leaves every future version-scoped view to re-implement the discipline. **(d) Publish `created_at` on `Model`** — orthogonal to the filter and worth naming here, since it is what would make "most recent" a readable property rather than an artefact of row ordering. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (a), with (d) alongside** |  |  |  |
| OQ-611 | open question |  | ~~**`GET /api/v1/models` cannot filter by dataset version**~~, so a view scoped to one — the factor workbench — filters in the client over a paginated list. A filter applied to one page renders "no models" while matches sit on a later page, indistinguishable from a version that has none. `OQ-605`'s shape one route over, and the only other recorded instance. A quieter consequence: `Model` carries **no timestamp**, so "most recent" exists only as the route's `ORDER BY id DESC` over UUIDv7 ids — a client that filters must also preserve order, and nothing in the type system says so. Options are a `dataset_version_id` filter on the route, a route on the version, a bounded and disclosed client filter, or publishing `created_at` on `Model`; the route filter is recommended, with `created_at` alongside. **No owner** — `02` §5.1 or `model-schema` changes, and `WK-692` is closed. `W6b-5b` ships the bounded client filter. Raised 2026-08-25. **Open**, owned by the maintainer; options and evidence are in `docs/open-questions.md`. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (a), with (d) alongside** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-612 | open question |  | ~~**An all-`null` set of holdout strength ratios cannot be told from an artifact that predates them, and the two warrant opposite readings.** `TransparencyArtifactRow.payload` stores the artifact whole as JSONB and is insert-only (FR-43), so an artifact written before `W6b-5a` carries **no `holdout_strength_ratio` key at all**, while one written after carries the key with `null` wherever no quotient exists. In storage those differ. They stop differing at `platform/transparency.py`'s `to_artifact`, which validates the payload into `TransparencyArtifact` before it leaves the platform layer — both become `None`, and the API returns the model rather than the payload. The suggestion panel therefore sees one value for two facts: *this model was analysed before the ratio existed*, whose honest reading is "rebuild to compute it", and *the booster found no interaction structure at all*, whose honest reading is that there was nothing in sample either. Rendering the first as the second misleads in the direction that matters — an actuary told "no structure found" about a model that was simply never re-analysed discards a pair on evidence that does not exist. **The exposure is narrower than it first appears, and the narrowing is verified in the code rather than assumed.** `holdout_strength_ratio` sits on `ShapInteraction`, so it is per candidate pair, and `_ratio` returns `None` **iff the pair's in-sample `strength` is `0.0`**: the only production call site passes a dict built by `_pair_strengths` over the same encoding, so the `holdout is None` arm is unreachable there and a pair with positive in-sample strength always carries a float. **So any candidate carrying a float proves the artifact post-dates `W6b-5a`, and only an artifact whose candidates are *all* `null` is undecidable** — a case that coincides with the booster having found no interaction structure at all, reachable but rare. **That two-cause reading rests on the call site, not on the type**, and the distinction matters if the call graph grows: `_ratio`'s `holdout is None` arm is unreachable only because the one production caller passes a dict, and nothing in the signature prevents a future path passing `None`. Such a path would emit an all-`null` artifact whose candidates have **positive** in-sample strengths — a third cause, silently indistinguishable from a pre-`W6b-5a` one, and one that would falsify the narrowing above rather than merely add to it. Where `top_interactions` is empty there is no ambiguity either: that is LightGBM, and `interactions_available` already says why. Mirrored in `docs/specs/02-modelling.md` §10.~~ | **(a) An explicit capability flag on `ShapSummary`** — say `holdout_ratio_available`, false on an artifact built before the ratio existed. **This is the house answer to this exact shape.** `interactions_available` exists for the same reason and its own comment states the principle: `top_interactions` is "empty rather than absent, and the reason is in `interactions_available`: a missing list reads as 'no interactions found', which is a finding this backend cannot make". Absence-of-capability versus absence-of-finding is the same distinction, and the platform already decided it once. Costs a `model-schema` change and a contract regeneration. **(b) Distinguish key-absent from `null` in `to_artifact`** and surface it. Cheap, and it leaks the storage representation into the API — the panel would depend on a JSONB key's presence, which is not a fact the contract expresses. **(c) Read `TransparencyArtifact.created_at` in the panel** and compare against the release. Needs no schema change and encodes a release date in the UI, where it rots and where nobody looking at the panel will know to update it. **(d) Accept it, and word the panel so one message is true of both.** Costs nothing and gives up the distinction permanently. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (a) — holdout_ratio_available on ShapSummary, the interactions_available precedent** |  |  |  |
| OQ-612 | open question |  | ~~**An all-`null` set of holdout strength ratios cannot be told from an artifact that predates them.**~~ `TransparencyArtifactRow.payload` stores the artifact whole as JSONB and is insert-only, so a pre-`W6b-5a` artifact carries no `holdout_strength_ratio` key while a later one carries `null` where no quotient exists; `to_artifact` validates the payload into `TransparencyArtifact` before it leaves the platform layer, so both reach a panel as `None`. The two warrant opposite readings — "rebuild to compute it" against "the booster found no interaction structure" — and rendering the first as the second discards a pair on evidence that does not exist. **Narrowed:** `_ratio` returns `None` iff in-sample `strength` is `0.0`, so any candidate carrying a float proves the artifact is post-`W6b-5a` and only an all-`null` one is undecidable; that rests on the call site rather than the type. Recommended: an explicit capability flag, the house answer `interactions_available` already gives this shape. **No owner** — a `model-schema` change, and `WK-692` is closed. `W6b-5b` ships wording true of both causes. Raised 2026-08-25. **Open**, owned by the maintainer; options and evidence are in `docs/open-questions.md`. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (a) — holdout_ratio_available on ShapSummary, the interactions_available precedent** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-613 | open question |  | ~~**A surrogate's source is pinned by UUID while every address derived from its slug resolves to the source family's latest version — so a rendered or derived link can land on a version the surrogate does not approximate.**~~ | **(a) A companion `slug@version` pair on `GlmSpec` following `IntervalFor`** — joins `spec_hash` (FR-206), costs a contract field and a bump; **(b) Resolve the pin in the rendering surfaces only** — no schema change, and every slug-derived address still lands on latest; **(c) Document the slug-derived address as family-level** — cheapest, leaves a link that can disagree with the pin. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (a) — GlmSpec gains a companion slug@version address following IntervalFor, joining spec_hash; the id stays the lookup key** |  |  |  |
| OQ-613 | open question |  | ~~**A surrogate's source is pinned by UUID while every address derived from its slug resolves to the source family's latest version — so a rendered or derived link can land on a version the surrogate does not approximate.**~~ Raised 2026-08-26 (WK-664 decision maker) out of OQ-604's ruling. `GlmSpec.approximates_model_id` pins one model by UUID (model-schema `modelling.py`:1040) with no companion family-and-version address, the surrogate's slug is derived from the source *family* slug with `-approx` appended (FR-141), and `/models/:slug` resolves to the family's latest version — so the fidelity numbers a reader follows from the derived address can be those of a different version's predictions, the misreading FR-137(i) exists to prevent. `IntervalFor` already establishes the shape: the central model is "named by **id and version**" (FR-199/200, model-schema `modelling.py`:1279-1280). Options are (a) a companion `slug@version` pair on `GlmSpec` following `IntervalFor` — joins `spec_hash` (FR-206), costs a contract field and a bump; (b) resolve the pin in the rendering surfaces only — no schema change, and every slug-derived address still lands on latest; (c) document the slug-derived address as family-level — cheapest, leaves a link that can disagree with the pin. Recommendation: (a), because it makes the surrogate self-describing in the sense FR-141 already claims, at the cost of a contract field and a `spec_hash` bump; the id stays the lookup key. **Open**, owned by the maintainer; options and evidence are in `docs/open-questions.md`. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: (a) — GlmSpec gains a companion slug@version address following IntervalFor, joining spec_hash; the id stays the lookup key** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-614 | open question |  | ~~Does ZEN Engine expression evaluation preserve exact decimal semantics for money?~~ **RESOLVED 2026-08-14.** | Research established that `zen-types` represents `Variable::Number` as `rust_decimal::Decimal` (96-bit mantissa, ~28 significant digits) and that all node inputs, outputs and expression results use that type system. Engine arithmetic is exact decimal; option (b) — relying on integer minor units being exactly representable in float64 — is unnecessary. |  |  |  |
| OQ-614 | open question |  | ~~Does the ZEN Engine preserve exact decimal semantics for money?~~ **Resolved 2026-08-14** — it represents numbers as `rust_decimal::Decimal`, so engine arithmetic is exact and ADR-706 stands. The risk moved to the boundaries and is now specified as FR-273/274/275; the S1 spike is re-scoped, not cancelled. See [`research`](research/track-a-findings.md) F1. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-615 | open question |  | ~~Is `model_call` in `exact` mode viable within the 50 ms p99 budget?~~ **RESOLVED 2026-08-14 by spike S2.** | Measured: a 500-tree × 60-feature booster scores a single row at **p99 1.09 ms** including `DMatrix` construction (0.33 ms predict-only) — roughly 2 % of the budget. `nthread=1` beats all-cores at the tail (p99 1.09 vs 1.48 ms; max 4.5 vs 19.9 ms). *(Amended 2026-08-27, WK-668 — re-measured on the verification machine: p99 1.626 ms vs all-cores' 4.737 ms (max 6.143 ms vs 26.692 ms); `docs/research/w8-spike-resolution.md`. nthread=1 still 0.34x of all-cores at the tail — the original S2 order holds, though the absolute figures are higher on this machine. Still comfortably inside the 50 ms budget.)* |  |  |  |
| OQ-615 | open question |  | ~~Is `model_call` in `exact` mode viable inside the 50 ms p99 budget?~~ **RESOLVED 2026-08-14 by spike S2 — comfortably yes.** A 500-tree × 60-feature booster scores a single row at **p99 1.09 ms** including `DMatrix` construction (0.33 ms predict-only) — about 2 % of the budget. `nthread=1` beat all-cores at the tail (p99 1.09 vs 1.48 ms; max 4.5 vs 19.9 ms), so per-request single-threading is correct. **OQ-575 is therefore a genuine design choice, not one forced by latency.** *(Amended 2026-08-27, WK-668 — re-measured on the verification machine: p99 1.626 ms vs all-cores' 4.737 ms (max 6.143 ms vs 26.692 ms); `docs/research/w8-spike-resolution.md`. nthread=1 still 0.34x of all-cores at the tail — the original S2 order holds, though the absolute figures are higher on this machine. p99 1.626 ms is still 3.3 % of the 50 ms budget: still comfortably viable. Recorded in NFR-501, amended the same way.)* |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-616 | open question |  | ~~Rate tables as PostgreSQL rows or content-addressed parquet blobs?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-18: rows up to a configurable cell count, spilling to parquet above it, under one contract.** | Rows: SQL-diffable, joinable to exposure for FR-231 weighting, natural for the editor's paging. Blobs: consistent with dataset storage, cheap for very large tables (vehicle × area can reach millions of cells), but every diff and every exposure-weighted view becomes a compute job. |  |  |  |
| OQ-616 | open question |  | ~~Should rate tables live in PostgreSQL as rows or as content-addressed parquet blobs?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-18: rows to a configurable cell count, spilling to parquet above it under one contract — FR-232**, with `storage` recorded on the version and the diff degrading to a Job above the threshold. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-617 | open question |  | ~~Mid-term adjustments and cancellations — same algorithm with a `purpose`, or a separate path?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-18: the same algorithm for the risk price, with the pro-rata/refund/charge maths in a separately-versioned sub-graph mounted on `purpose`.** | Same algorithm: one structure to maintain and approve, but pro-rata, cancellation charges, and refund rules genuinely differ from new-business maths and would bloat every graph with MTA-only branches. Separate path: cleaner graphs, two things to keep consistent. |  |  |  |
| OQ-617 | open question |  | ~~How do mid-term adjustments and refunds work — a `purpose` on the same algorithm, or a genuinely separate calculation path?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-18: the same algorithm for the risk price, with pro-rata/refund/charge logic in a separately-versioned sub-graph mounted on `purpose` — FR-218.** §2's `purpose` gained `cancellation` in the same edit, because the answer keys on a value that did not exist. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-618 | open question |  | ~~Multi-product bundling in Phase 2?~~ | In-platform: needed for a genuine bundle discount priced on combined risk, requires a multi-product Quote Context and cross-product rating versions. Left to the Consumer System: much simpler, but the bundle discount is then unpriced and unmonitored. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: deferral confirmed — Phase 4; Consumer System bundling is the supported pattern** |  |  |  |
| OQ-618 | open question |  | ~~Do we support multi-product bundling (motor + home in one quote with a bundle discount) in Phase 2, or is each product a separate Rating Version with bundling left to the Consumer System?~~ **Deferred to Phase 4**: Phase 2 ships single-product Rating Versions and a Consumer System bundling two quotes is a supported pattern — the bundle discount is then unpriced and unmonitored, and cross-product pricing follows the optimisation work that needs the same joint demand modelling. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: deferral confirmed — Phase 4; Consumer System bundling is the supported pattern** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-619 | open question |  | ~~Does the platform own instalment/APR calculation?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-18: price the annual premium, offer `instalment_loading` as an optional final ladder rung, and leave APR and schedule generation downstream.** | It changes the price the customer actually compares, so the demand model in `04` is fitted on the wrong price without it. But it is billing-domain logic with its own regulatory surface (consumer credit). |  |  |  |
| OQ-619 | open question |  | ~~Should the platform own instalment/APR calculation, or is that a downstream billing concern?~~ **DECIDED 2026-08-18: price the annual premium, offer `instalment_loading` as a final ladder rung, and leave APR and schedules downstream — FR-252.** Enough for `04`'s demand model; not enough to make a rating release a consumer-credit release. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-620 | open question |  | Does a **Rate Table Version** have an approval lifecycle — is `06` right that it is a Governed Artifact, or are `03` §3.3 and the schema right that it has no status? | **(a) `03` and the code are incomplete** — add `draft → review → approved`, a status column and migration, a `06` §4.2 policy entry and an `EVIDENCE_FLOOR` key; the compile gate then means something for the artifact that most directly sets the price, at the cost of a migration on a shipped table and a retro-approval decision for existing versions. **(b) `06` is the wrong side** — a rate table is governed through the Rating Version that pins it, and `06` §3.3's Rating Version row already requires *"rate table diffs"*, so approving both approves one change twice on the most frequently versioned artifact in the platform (FR-233 bulk operations and FR-235 imports each mint versions); costs striking Rate Table Version from `06` §2 and §3.3. **(c) Split by phase** — ungoverned in Phase 2, a lifecycle in Phase 3 with WK-677; defers rather than answers and leaves `06` untrue for a phase. |  |  |  |
| OQ-620 | open question |  | Does a **Rate Table Version** have an approval lifecycle? `06` §2 lists it as a Governed Artifact — *"any artifact with an approval-bearing lifecycle"* — and `06` §3.3 gives it required evidence at submission; this section gives it no status at all and `rate_table_versions` has no status column, so `compile_bundle`'s FR-20 maturity gate has nothing to read for a `rate_table` pin. **Open.** *(Raised 2026-08-29 from WK-671 Task 1.2, which needed a maturity to report and found none; the pin is exempted from the gate meanwhile, declared and guarded so a status column turns the exemption red. Ruled in `docs/rulings/RL-00856-the-resolver-reports-no-maturity-for-a-rate-table-and-the-exemption-is-declared-and-self-invalidating.md` RL-856.)* |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-621 | open question |  | ~~Which solver family?~~ | SLSQP (`scipy`): simple, no extra dependency, adequate for a few hundred smooth segment factors, degrades badly at thousands of variables and with non-smooth constraints. `cvxpy` + a conic solver: far more robust, gives genuine infeasibility certificates and shadow prices for free (FR-288), but constrains objectives to convex forms — which excludes some blends users will want. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: deferral confirmed — SLSQP in Phase 4; the cvxpy revisit trigger stands** |  |  |  |
| OQ-621 | open question |  | ~~Which solver family? SLSQP is simple and adequate for smooth segment-factor problems but struggles at scale and with non-smooth constraints; a dedicated conic/LP formulation (via `cvxpy`) is far more robust for linear constraints but restricts the objective forms we can express.~~ **Deferred to Phase 4**: start with SLSQP for the segment-factor formulation and revisit `cvxpy` if segment counts exceed ~1 000 or infeasibility diagnosis (NFR-506) proves unreliable. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: deferral confirmed — SLSQP in Phase 4; the cvxpy revisit trigger stands** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-622 | open question |  | ~~Competitor position: first-class concept or just a dataset column?~~ | First-class: enables market-response modelling, needs a competitor data model, ingestion cadence, and matching logic we cannot validate without the data. Column: zero platform cost, user does the joining, but then "rank in market" cannot be a constraint or a monitored metric. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: dataset column in Phases 1-4, the convention documented** |  |  |  |
| OQ-622 | open question |  | ~~Does the platform ship a competitor-position capability (rank in market, price vs cheapest) as a first-class concept, or is it just another dataset column? First-class means modelling market response; a column means the user does the work.~~ **Open** — the recommendation is a documented dataset column in Phases 1–4, promotable to first-class later without a migration; options and trade-offs are in `docs/open-questions.md`. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: dataset column in Phases 1-4, the convention documented** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-623 | open question |  | ~~Expected claims in the profit objective — platform models or externally supplied?~~ | Platform models: internally consistent, everything traces to an approved artifact. External (reserving-derived): matches what the finance function believes, and will differ from our models in ways that get noticed in a pricing committee. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: support both — the profit definition names its expected-claims source, never a default** |  |  |  |
| OQ-623 | open question |  | ~~Is `expected_profit` computed with the platform's own risk models, or should it accept an externally-supplied expected loss cost? Insurers often have a separate reserving-derived view that will not match our models, and the mismatch will be noticed.~~ **Open** — the recommendation is to support both, with the profit definition (FR-285) naming its expected-claims source; options and trade-offs are in `docs/open-questions.md`. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: support both — the profit definition names its expected-claims source, never a default** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-624 | open question |  | ~~How is demand-model endogeneity handled?~~ | Historic prices were set by a structure already conditioned on risk, so naive elasticity is biased toward zero (or worse). Options: instrumental variables (needs a valid instrument, rarely available), randomised price testing (see OQ-625), or documenting the caveat. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: document prominently and quantify; identification_note required on every Demand Model** |  |  |  |
| OQ-624 | open question |  | ~~How is the demand model's endogeneity problem handled? Historic prices were set by a rating structure that already conditioned on risk, so a naive elasticity estimate is biased. Do we specify instrumental variables, a randomised price-test capability, or simply document the caveat prominently?~~ **Open** — the recommendation is to document and quantify via FR-278's price-variation report, with an `identification_note` required on every Demand Model; real identification needs OQ-625. Options and trade-offs are in `docs/open-questions.md`. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: document prominently and quantify; identification_note required on every Demand Model** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-625 | open question |  | ~~Should the platform support randomised **price testing** on live traffic?~~ | It is the only clean answer to OQ-624, and it means deliberately quoting some customers a different price to learn from it — operationally involved and ethically loaded, with FCA fair-value implications. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: deferral confirmed — post-Phase 4, with the price_test capability boundary specified now** |  |  |  |
| OQ-625 | open question |  | ~~Should the platform support~~ **price testing** (randomised price variation on a slice of live traffic) to generate the variation FR-278 needs? It is the correct answer to OQ-624 and is operationally and ethically loaded. **Deferred (post-Phase 4)**: not built in Phases 1–4 — deliberately quoting some customers a different price to learn from it has FCA fair-value implications — but the capability boundary (a `price_test` purpose the platform refuses to serve) is specified now, so an eventual addition is deliberate and governed rather than emergent. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: deferral confirmed — post-Phase 4, with the price_test capability boundary specified now** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-626 | open question |  | ~~GIPP: what is the correct ENBP when channels differ?~~ **RESOLVED 2026-08-14.** | Not a judgement call — **the rule prescribes it.** ICOBS 6B.2.5R(1) requires the channel the customer used when they **first purchased** the policy; 6B.2.5R(2) falls back to the channel most commonly used by new-business customers where the original is unavailable or unidentifiable. |  |  |  |
| OQ-626 | open question |  | ~~For GIPP, what is the correct treatment of channel differences?~~ **RESOLVED 2026-08-14 — the rule prescribes it.** ICOBS 6B.2.5R(1) requires the channel the customer used when they *first purchased* the policy, with 6B.2.5R(2) falling back to the channel most commonly used by new-business customers. This is now FR-294/295, and it corrected a design that keyed off the *renewal* channel. |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-627 | open question |  | ~~Is 1 % trace sampling enough for segment-level A/E?~~ | At 1 % of 50 M quotes, a segment holding 0.5 % of business gets ~2 500 quotes/year — far too thin for a per-level A/E with usable CIs. Options: raise the rate (storage, NFR-500), stratify sampling by segment (complex, biases need correcting), or compute A/E from a **full batch re-score** of the exposure dataset rather than from traces. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: full batch re-score of the exposure dataset for A/E; traces stay for quote-level metrics** |  |  |  |
| OQ-627 | open question |  | ~~Is a 1 % trace sample sufficient for segment-level A/E monitoring? At 1 % of 50 M quotes, thin segments have too few observations to say anything. Options: raise the sample rate, stratify sampling by segment, or compute A/E from a full batch re-score of the exposure dataset instead of from traces.~~ **Open** — the recommendation is the full batch re-score (FR-253), keeping traces for quote-level metrics; options and trade-offs are in `docs/open-questions.md`. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: full batch re-score of the exposure dataset for A/E; traces stay for quote-level metrics** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-628 | open question |  | ~~PostgreSQL or a dedicated time-series store for monitoring metrics?~~ | Postgres: one fewer system, adequate at weekly cadence, partitioning handles growth. Dedicated TSDB (Timescale/Prometheus): better for daily per-replica operational metrics, another system to run and back up (NFR-462 wants a simple local stack). **DECIDED 2026-08-26: PostgreSQL with declarative partitioning; no TSDB in the required stack** |  |  |  |
| OQ-628 | open question |  | ~~Do we store monitoring time series in PostgreSQL or adopt a dedicated time-series store? Postgres is simpler and adequate at weekly cadence; daily operational metrics at per-replica granularity may outgrow it.~~ **Open** — the recommendation is PostgreSQL with declarative partitioning, no dedicated TSDB; options and trade-offs are in `docs/open-questions.md`. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: PostgreSQL with declarative partitioning; no TSDB in the required stack** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-629 | open question |  | ~~Who owns the claims-development pattern used for maturity adjustment?~~ | User-supplied reference data: simple, defers the actuarial work to the user, and the pattern is exactly what a reserving team already has. Platform-fitted from triangles: better UX, meaningful new scope (triangle construction, tail fitting), overlaps reserving which is explicitly out of scope. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: user-supplied as a Reference Table (development_pattern), referenced by maturity_policy** |  |  |  |
| OQ-629 | open question |  | ~~Who owns the claims-development pattern used for maturity adjustment (R4) — is it supplied by the user as reference data, fitted by the platform from claim triangles, or out of scope (tied to `01` OQ-560)?~~ **Open** — the recommendation is user-supplied reference data (`development_pattern`), tying off with `01` OQ-560; options and trade-offs are in `docs/open-questions.md`. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: user-supplied as a Reference Table (development_pattern), referenced by maturity_policy** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-630 | open question |  | ~~Automated champion/challenger on live traffic?~~ | It measures what shadow scoring cannot — realised conversion and claims under the challenger — but it means real customers get the challenger price, which is a live pricing experiment with fair-value implications (same family as OQ-625). **DECIDED 2026-08-26: deferral confirmed — shadow scoring only, grouped with OQ-625** |  |  |  |
| OQ-630 | open question |  | ~~Should the platform support automated~~ **champion/challenger** — routing a slice of live traffic to a candidate Rating Version and comparing realised outcomes — or is shadow scoring (no customer impact) the ethical and practical limit? **Deferred (post-Phase 4)**: shadow scoring only (FR-271) — routing real customers to a challenger price is a live pricing experiment with fair-value implications, the same governed decision as price testing, and it is grouped with OQ-625 rather than decided separately. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: deferral confirmed — shadow scoring only, grouped with OQ-625** |  |  |  |  |
| OQ-631 | open question |  | ~~What is the right default cadence per metric family?~~ | One cadence for everything is wrong in both directions: weekly A/E is noise on a low-frequency peril, monthly drift is far too slow to catch a broken feed. **DECIDED 2026-08-26: per family: input_drift daily, operational hourly, price_portfolio weekly, demand weekly, model_ae monthly with a 6-month maturity floor** |  |  |  |
